Learn English/Parts of speech/04-adjectives/catalog

More adjectives: T–U#

Adjectives T–U beyond the grammar groups — the everyday adjectives that a triage of WordNet’s adjective lemmas showed a learner needs.

← The grammar of adjectives · All groups

Most English adjectives behave: they go before a noun or after be, and they compare with -er or more. These are those — but each still says how it compares, where it can stand, and what it takes. WordNet lists about twenty thousand adjectives; most of the rest are technical, archaic or British-only, and a triage pass left them out on purpose.

taboo#

Pronunciation
/təˈbuː/ · tuh-BOO
Русский
запретный, табуированный
Type
classifying; also quality
Position
both — a taboo subject, Money talk is taboo here
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Also a noun: breaking a taboo. The stress is on the second syllable: tuh-BOO.

Not allowed to be talked about or done, by custom.

  1. Money is still a taboo subject in many families.
  2. In his house, politics at dinner was taboo.
  3. The play deals with taboo topics.

tacit#

Pronunciation
/ˈtæsɪt/ · TAS-it
Русский
молчаливый, негласный
Type
classifying; also quality
Position
attributive only — before a noun: tacit approval
Comparison
not gradable
Form
from Latin tacere, to be silent
Opposite
explicit, stated
Contrast
Tacit means understood without being said. It has nothing to do with tact — that word gives tactful and tactless.

Understood or agreed to without being said out loud.

  1. The plan had the boss's tacit approval.
  2. There was a tacit understanding that nobody would mention it.
  3. Silence here counts as tacit consent.

taciturn#

Pronunciation
/ˈtæsɪtɜːrn/ · TAS-i-turn
Русский
молчаливый, неразговорчивый
Type
quality
Position
both — a taciturn man, He gets taciturn
Comparison
more/most — more taciturn, most taciturn
Opposite
talkative

Saying very little by nature.

  1. Her father was a taciturn man who answered in one word.
  2. He gets taciturn when he's working something out.
  3. The two taciturn brothers ran the farm together.

tacky#

Pronunciation
/ˈtæki/ · TAK-ee
Русский
безвкусный, пошлый; липкий
Type
evaluative; also quality
Position
both — a tacky souvenir, The paint is still tacky
Comparison
-er/-est — tackier, tackiest

Tacky has 2 distinct senses.

1. Cheap-looking and in poor taste. (безвкусный, пошлый)

  1. The lobby is full of tacky gold furniture.
  2. It felt tacky to bring up money at the funeral.
  3. Every year their holiday sweaters get tackier.

2. Slightly sticky, like paint that isn't dry yet. (липкий, ещё не высохший)

  1. The varnish is still tacky.
  2. The floor felt tacky under our shoes.

tactful#

Pronunciation
/ˈtæktfəl/ · TAKT-fuhl
Русский
тактичный, деликатный
Type
quality; also evaluative
Position
both — a tactful answer, She was tactful about it
Comparison
more/most — more tactful, most tactful
Takes
about + noun (the touchy subject)
Form
-ful from tact
Opposite
tactless

Careful not to say the thing that would hurt or embarrass someone.

  1. She found a tactful way to say no.
  2. He's honest but never tactful.
  3. It wasn't tactful to bring up his ex.

tactical#

Pronunciation
/ˈtæktɪkəl/ · TAK-ti-kuhl
Русский
тактический
Type
classifying
Position
both — mostly before a noun: a tactical error
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-al from tactic
Contrast
Tactical is about the short-term moves; strategic is about the long-term plan: a tactical retreat serves a strategic goal.

Tactical has 2 distinct senses.

1. Having to do with the moves you make to reach a goal. (тактический)

  1. Pulling out of the deal was a tactical mistake.
  2. The coach made a tactical decision to rest him.

2. Used on the battlefield, at short range — of weapons and units. (тактический (о вооружении))

  1. The unit trains for tactical operations in cities.
  2. Tactical nuclear weapons have a short range.

tactile#

Pronunciation
/ˈtæktəl/ · TAK-tuhl
Русский
осязательный, тактильный
Type
classifying
Position
both — mostly before a noun: tactile feedback
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Also said TAK-tyl. American -ile words vary: fragile is FRAJ-uhl, mobile is MOH-buhl.

Tactile has 2 distinct senses.

1. Having to do with the sense of touch. (осязательный, тактильный)

  1. Babies learn through tactile exploration.
  2. A glass screen gives you no tactile feedback.

2. Pleasant or interesting to touch. (приятный на ощупь)

  1. The book has a lovely tactile cover.
  2. She likes tactile fabrics like velvet and raw wool.

tactless#

Pronunciation
/ˈtæktləs/ · TAKT-luhs
Русский
бестактный
Type
evaluative
Position
both — a tactless remark, That was tactless
Comparison
more/most — more tactless, most tactless
Form
-less from tact
Opposite
tactful

Saying the wrong thing without thinking how it will land.

  1. That was a tactless thing to say to a new widow.
  2. He isn't cruel, just tactless.
  3. Her tactless question silenced the table.

Tahitian#

Pronunciation
/təˈhiːʃən/ · tuh-HEE-shuhn
Русский
таитянский
Type
origin
Position
both — Tahitian pearls, Her mother is Tahitian
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-an from Tahiti

Of or from Tahiti, its people or its language.

  1. We stayed in a Tahitian village for a week.
  2. She collects Tahitian pearls.
  3. The hotel serves a Tahitian breakfast of fruit and coconut.

tailor-made#

Pronunciation
/ˌteɪlərˈmeɪd/ · tay-lur-MAYD
Русский
сшитый на заказ; идеально подходящий
Type
compound
Position
both — a tailor-made suit, The job is tailor-made for her
Comparison
not gradable
Takes
for + noun or person (who it suits)
Form
compound — noun + past participle
Opposite
ready-made, off-the-rack

Tailor-made has 2 distinct senses.

1. Made to fit one particular person, of clothes. (сшитый на заказ)

  1. He wore a tailor-made suit to the hearing.
  2. Her winter coat is tailor-made.

2. Perfectly suited to a person or a purpose. (будто созданный, идеально подходящий)

  1. The job is tailor-made for her.
  2. It was a tailor-made excuse to leave early.

tailored#

Pronunciation
/ˈteɪlərd/ · TAY-lurd
Русский
приталенный; подогнанный под нужды
Type
participial
Position
both — a tailored jacket, The course is tailored to beginners
Comparison
not gradable
Takes
to + noun (whose needs it fits)
Form
-ed from tailor

Tailored has 2 distinct senses.

1. Cut to fit closely and neatly, of clothes. (приталенный, хорошо сидящий)

  1. She wore a tailored gray jacket.
  2. The pants are tailored and a little too tight.

2. Made or changed to fit a particular need. (подогнанный, адаптированный)

  1. The lessons are tailored to each student.
  2. We offer plans tailored to small businesses.

tainted#

Pronunciation
/ˈteɪntɪd/ · TAYN-tid
Русский
заражённый, испорченный; запятнанный
Type
participial
Position
both — tainted meat, The evidence was tainted
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ed from taint

Tainted has 2 distinct senses.

1. Spoiled or made unsafe by something harmful in it. (заражённый, испорченный)

  1. Dozens of people got sick from tainted lettuce.
  2. The well water was tainted with gasoline.

2. Damaged in its reputation or fairness. (запятнанный, скомпрометированный)

  1. The verdict was tainted by the leaked photos.
  2. He left office with a tainted record.

Taiwanese#

Pronunciation
/ˌtaɪwɑːˈniːz/ · ty-wah-NEEZ
Русский
тайваньский
Type
origin
Position
both — Taiwanese food, The company is Taiwanese
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ese from Taiwan
Contrast
Like all -ese words it adds no -s for people: three Taiwanese engineers. For one person say a Taiwanese woman, not a Taiwanese.

Of or from Taiwan, its people or its culture.

  1. We had Taiwanese beef noodle soup.
  2. The chip company is Taiwanese.
  3. Taiwanese students make up a big part of the program.

take-home#

Pronunciation
/ˈteɪkˌhoʊm/ · TAYK-hohm
Русский
чистый (о зарплате); на дом
Type
compound
Position
attributive only — before a noun: take-home pay
Comparison
not gradable
Form
compound — verb + adverb

Take-home has 2 distinct senses.

1. Left over after taxes are taken out, of pay. (чистый, на руки)

  1. My take-home pay is about two-thirds of that number.
  2. The raise barely changed her take-home amount.

2. Meant to be taken away and used or done at home. (на дом, с собой)

  1. The final is a take-home exam.
  2. The take-home message is simple: start early.

taken#

Pronunciation
/ˈteɪkən/ · TAY-kuhn
Русский
занятый; увлечённый
Type
participial
Position
predicative only — after a verb: That seat is taken
Comparison
not gradable — but very taken with is common
Takes
with or by + noun (what charmed you)
Form
past participle of take
Contrast
Of a person, He's taken means he is already in a relationship.

Taken has 2 distinct senses.

1. Already used, occupied or claimed. (занятый)

  1. Sorry, this seat is taken.
  2. Every decent username was already taken.

2. Charmed or impressed by someone or something. (очарованный, увлечённый)

  1. She was quite taken with the little house.
  2. I'm not taken with the new design.

takeout#

Pronunciation
/ˈteɪkˌaʊt/ · TAYK-owt
Русский
на вынос
Type
compound; also classifying
Position
attributive only — before a noun: takeout food
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Also a noun: Let's get takeout tonight. The British say takeaway. Written as one word for the adjective, two for the verb: take out the trash.

Sold to be carried away and eaten somewhere else.

  1. The fridge is full of takeout containers.
  2. We ordered takeout pizza and watched a movie.
  3. The place has a takeout window on the side.

talented#

Pronunciation
/ˈtæləntɪd/ · TAL-uhn-tid
Русский
талантливый
Type
quality; also evaluative
Position
both — a talented singer, The cast is talented
Comparison
more/most — more talented, most talented
Takes
at + noun or -ing
Form
-ed from talent
Opposite
talentless

Having a natural ability to do something well.

  1. She's a talented cellist.
  2. He's the most talented kid in the program.
  3. The cast is young and talented.

talentless#

Pronunciation
/ˈtæləntləs/ · TAL-uhnt-luhs
Русский
бездарный
Type
evaluative
Position
both — a talentless hack, The band is talentless
Comparison
more/most — more talentless, most talentless
Form
-less from talent
Opposite
talented

Having no skill or ability at all.

  1. The critics called the band talentless.
  2. He isn't talentless — he just never practices.
  3. It's a talentless remake of a good movie.

talkative#

Pronunciation
/ˈtɔːkətɪv/ · TAW-kuh-tiv
Русский
разговорчивый, болтливый
Type
quality
Position
both — a talkative kid, He gets talkative
Comparison
more/most — more talkative, most talkative
Form
-ative from talk
Opposite
quiet, taciturn
Contrast
Talkative is neutral, chatty is warm, long-winded is the unkind one.

Talking a lot, or liking to talk.

  1. He gets talkative after one beer.
  2. She's a talkative kid who narrates everything she does.
  3. The driver wasn't talkative, which was fine with me.

tall#

Pronunciation
/tɔːl/ · TAWL
Русский
высокий
Type
size
Position
both — a tall building, He's tall
Comparison
-er/-est — taller, tallest
Opposite
short
Contrast
Tall is for people, trees, buildings and other narrow things; mountains and flying planes are high. With a measurement the adjective comes last: six feet tall, never tall six feet.

Tall has 2 distinct senses.

1. Of more than average height. (высокий, рослый)

  1. Her brother is very tall.
  2. They planted tall grass along the fence.
  3. He's the tallest player on the team.

2. Hard to believe or hard to do — in fixed phrases. (невероятный; непосильный)

  1. Getting there by six is a tall order.
  2. Grandpa told us another tall tale about the war.

tame#

Pronunciation
/teɪm/ · TAYM
Русский
ручной, прирученный; пресный, вялый
Type
quality
Position
both — a tame rabbit, The movie is pretty tame
Comparison
-er/-est — tamer, tamest
Opposite
wild
Contrast
Tame is also a verb: They tamed the horse in a week.

Tame has 2 distinct senses.

1. Not wild — used to people and not dangerous. (ручной, прирученный)

  1. The deer in the park are almost tame.
  2. She kept a tame crow that rode on her shoulder.

2. Milder or duller than you expected. (пресный, безобидный)

  1. The movie is pretty tame for an R rating.
  2. The party was tamer than last year.

Tamil#

Pronunciation
/ˈtæməl/ · TAM-uhl
Русский
тамильский
Type
origin
Position
both — a Tamil family, Her husband is Tamil
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Always capitalized, and the same word names the people and the language: Tamil food, She speaks Tamil.

Of the Tamil people of southern India and Sri Lanka, or their language.

  1. He grew up in a Tamil neighborhood in Chennai.
  2. The restaurant serves Tamil food.
  3. Tamil movies are big business.

tan#

Pronunciation
/tæn/ · TAN
Русский
жёлто-коричневый; загорелый
Type
color
Position
both — tan pants, She's really tan
Comparison
-er/-est or more/most — tanner, tannest, or more tan, most tan
Contrast
Double the n: tanner, tannest. Tan is also a verb (I burn, I don't tan) and a noun (She came back with a great tan).

Tan has 2 distinct senses.

1. Of a light yellowish-brown color. (светло-коричневый, песочный)

  1. He wore tan khakis and a blue shirt.
  2. They picked a tan carpet for the hallway.

2. Having skin darkened by the sun. (загорелый)

  1. She came back from Florida tan and rested.
  2. He's tanner now than he was in May.

tangential#

Pronunciation
/tænˈdʒɛnʃəl/ · tan-JEN-shuhl
Русский
побочный, не относящийся к делу
Type
classifying; also quality
Position
both — mostly after a verb, with to
Comparison
more/most — more tangential, most tangential
Takes
to + noun (the main subject)
Form
-al from tangent

Only slightly connected to the main subject.

  1. That point is tangential to what we're discussing.
  2. He kept going off on tangential stories about his cousin.
  3. Her role in the project was tangential at best.

tangible#

Pronunciation
/ˈtændʒəbəl/ · TAN-juh-buhl
Русский
осязаемый, ощутимый
Type
quality
Position
both — tangible results, The benefit is tangible
Comparison
more/most — more tangible, most tangible
Form
-ible from Latin tangere, to touch
Opposite
intangible

Tangible has 2 distinct senses.

1. Real and clear enough to be seen, measured or pointed to. (ощутимый, реальный)

  1. We need tangible results by June.
  2. There's no tangible evidence of a leak.

2. Able to be touched. (осязаемый)

  1. The tension in the room was almost tangible.
  2. Cash in your hand is tangible in a way a number on a screen isn't.

tangled#

Pronunciation
/ˈtæŋɡəld/ · TANG-guhld
Русский
запутанный, спутанный
Type
participial
Position
both — tangled hair, The cords got tangled
Comparison
more/most — more tangled, most tangled
Form
-ed from tangle

Tangled has 2 distinct senses.

1. Twisted together into a knot or a mess. (спутанный, запутавшийся)

  1. Her hair was tangled from the wind.
  2. The headphones came out of my bag tangled again.

2. Confusingly complicated. (запутанный, сложный)

  1. It's a tangled story with too many people in it.
  2. The company sits behind a tangled web of subsidiaries.

tangy#

Pronunciation
/ˈtæŋi/ · TANG-ee
Русский
терпкий, с кислинкой
Type
quality
Position
both — a tangy sauce, The dressing is tangy
Comparison
-er/-est — tangier, tangiest
Form
-y from tang

Having a strong, pleasantly sharp or sour taste or smell.

  1. The sauce is sweet and tangy.
  2. I like a tangy goat cheese on that salad.
  3. This lemonade is tangier than the last batch.

tanned#

Pronunciation
/tænd/ · TAND
Русский
загорелый
Type
participial
Position
both — tanned arms, He came back tanned
Comparison
more/most — more tanned, most tanned
Form
-ed from tan
Contrast
Tanned and tan both describe sun-darkened skin, and Americans often just say tan: She's very tan. Leather is tanned too — that is the older meaning.

Having skin made browner by the sun.

  1. He came back from Greece tanned and relaxed.
  2. Her tanned arms stood out against the white shirt.
  3. Everyone on the boat was tanned by the second day.

tantalizing#

Pronunciation
/ˈtæntəlaɪzɪŋ/ · TAN-tuh-ly-zing
Русский
дразнящий, соблазнительный
Type
participial; also evaluative
Position
both — a tantalizing hint, The smell was tantalizing
Comparison
more/most — more tantalizing, most tantalizing
Form
-ing from tantalize, after King Tantalus
Contrast
American spelling is -izing; tantalising is British.

Attractive in a way that makes you want something you can't quite have.

  1. The smell from the kitchen was tantalizing.
  2. She dropped a tantalizing hint about the ending.
  3. The offer was tantalizing, but the timing was wrong.

tantamount#

Pronunciation
/ˈtæntəmaʊnt/ · TAN-tuh-mownt
Русский
равносильный
Type
quality
Position
predicative only — after a verb, always with to
Comparison
not gradable
Takes
to + noun or -ing
Preposition patterns
tantamount to

Equal in effect to something bad, even if it isn't called that.

  1. Saying nothing here is tantamount to agreeing.
  2. The memo was tantamount to an admission of guilt.
  3. Refusing the test is tantamount to a confession.

Tanzanian#

Pronunciation
/ˌtænzəˈniːən/ · tan-zuh-NEE-uhn
Русский
танзанийский
Type
origin
Position
both — the Tanzanian coast, Our guide is Tanzanian
Comparison

-an from Tanzania

Of or from Tanzania or its people.

  1. The Tanzanian coast is beautiful in February.
  2. Our guide was Tanzanian.
  3. She works with a Tanzanian coffee cooperative.

Taoist#

Pronunciation
/ˈdaʊɪst/ · DOW-ist
Русский
даосский
Type
origin
Position
both — a Taoist temple, His teacher is Taoist
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ist from Tao
Contrast
Usually said with a d: DOW-ist, rhyming with now; TOW-ist is also heard. The newer spelling is Daoist, and the same word names a follower.

Of Taoism, the Chinese philosophy and religion, or its followers.

  1. We visited a Taoist temple outside the city.
  2. He studies Taoist philosophy.
  3. The book explains Taoist ideas about nature.

tapered#

Pronunciation
/ˈteɪpərd/ · TAY-purd
Русский
сужающийся, конусообразный
Type
participial; also shape
Position
both — mostly before a noun: tapered jeans
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ed from taper

Getting narrower toward one end.

  1. He wears tapered jeans now.
  2. The candle has a tapered top.
  3. The legs of the table are gently tapered.

tardy#

Pronunciation
/ˈtɑːrdi/ · TAR-dee
Русский
опоздавший; запоздалый
Type
quality
Position
both — a tardy student, He was tardy again
Comparison
-er/-est — tardier, tardiest
Opposite
prompt, punctual
Contrast
In American schools it is the official word for late, and it is even a noun there: three tardies and you get detention.

Tardy has 2 distinct senses.

1. Arriving later than you should. (опоздавший)

  1. He was tardy twice this week.
  2. Tardy students have to check in at the office.

2. Coming later than it should have. (запоздалый)

  1. The city's response was tardy and half-hearted.
  2. Her thank-you notes get tardier every year.

tart#

Pronunciation
/tɑːrt/ · TART
Русский
кислый, терпкий; язвительный
Type
quality
Position
both — a tart apple, These cherries are tart
Comparison
-er/-est — tarter, tartest
Contrast
Tart is also a noun — a small open fruit pie.

Tart has 2 distinct senses.

1. Pleasantly sour in taste. (кислый, с кислинкой)

  1. These cherries are tart.
  2. A tart apple works better in a pie.
  3. The green ones are tarter than the red.

2. Sharp and a little unkind, of a remark or a tone. (язвительный, колкий)

  1. She gave a tart reply and hung up.
  2. His reviews are famous for their tart humor.

Tasmanian#

Pronunciation
/tæzˈmeɪniən/ · taz-MAY-nee-uhn
Русский
тасманийский
Type
origin
Position
both — the Tasmanian coast, Her mother is Tasmanian
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-an from Tasmania
Contrast
The Tasmanian devil is a real animal about the size of a small dog, not only a cartoon.

Of or from Tasmania, the island state of Australia.

  1. The Tasmanian devil hunts at night.
  2. We hiked along the Tasmanian coast.
  3. Her mother is Tasmanian.

tasseled#

Pronunciation
/ˈtæsəld/ · TAS-uhld
Русский
с кисточками
Type
participial
Position
attributive only — before a noun: tasseled loafers
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ed from tassel
Contrast
American spelling keeps one l: tasseled; British doubles it, tasselled.

Decorated with tassels.

  1. He wore tasseled loafers with no socks.
  2. A tasseled lamp shade sat on the piano.
  3. The graduates threw their tasseled caps in the air.

tasteful#

Pronunciation
/ˈteɪstfəl/ · TAYST-fuhl
Русский
со вкусом сделанный, изысканный
Type
evaluative
Position
both — a tasteful room, The tribute was tasteful
Comparison
more/most — more tasteful, most tasteful
Form
-ful from taste
Opposite
tasteless, tacky
Contrast
Tasteful is about good taste in style, never about flavor — food that tastes good is tasty.

Showing good judgment about style or about what is appropriate.

  1. The apartment is small but tasteful.
  2. They sent a tasteful arrangement of white flowers.
  3. He made a tasteful joke about it, which is harder than it sounds.

tasteless#

Pronunciation
/ˈteɪstləs/ · TAYST-luhs
Русский
пошлый, бестактный; безвкусный (о еде)
Type
evaluative; also quality
Position
both — a tasteless joke, The soup was tasteless
Comparison
more/most — more tasteless, most tasteless
Form
-less from taste
Opposite
tasteful; tasty (of food)

Tasteless has 2 distinct senses.

1. Offensive or in bad taste. (пошлый, бестактный)

  1. He told a tasteless joke about the accident.
  2. The ad was widely called tasteless.

2. Having no flavor at all. (безвкусный, пресный)

  1. The soup was warm and tasteless.
  2. Hospital food is famously tasteless.

tasty#

Pronunciation
/ˈteɪsti/ · TAY-stee
Русский
вкусный
Type
evaluative; also quality
Position
both — a tasty sandwich, That was tasty
Comparison
-er/-est — tastier, tastiest
Form
-y from taste
Opposite
tasteless, bland
Contrast
Tasty is about flavor; tasteful is about style. For Russian вкусный Americans most often just say really good: This soup is really good.

Having a good flavor.

  1. That was a tasty sandwich.
  2. She makes a tasty black bean soup.
  3. The second batch was tastier than the first.

tattered#

Pronunciation
/ˈtætərd/ · TAT-urd
Русский
потрёпанный, изорванный
Type
participial
Position
both — mostly before a noun: a tattered flag
Comparison
more/most — more tattered, most tattered
Form
-ed from tatter

Tattered has 2 distinct senses.

1. Old and torn. (изорванный, потрёпанный)

  1. He carried a tattered paperback everywhere.
  2. The flag over the post office is tattered.

2. Badly damaged — of a reputation or a relationship. (подорванный, изрядно потрёпанный)

  1. The deal left his reputation tattered.
  2. They're trying to repair a tattered friendship.

taupe#

Pronunciation
/toʊp/ · TOHP
Русский
серо-коричневый, тауп
Type
color
Position
both — a taupe rug, The walls are taupe
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
One syllable, like tope: TOHP. It names the gray-brown between beige and gray, and it is also a noun: a soft taupe.

Of a grayish-brown color.

  1. The walls in the hallway are taupe.
  2. She picked a taupe rug for the bedroom.
  3. Taupe curtains warm up a gray room.

taut#

Pronunciation
/tɔːt/ · TAWT
Русский
натянутый, тугой; напряжённый
Type
quality
Position
both — a taut rope, Pull the line taut
Comparison
-er/-est — tauter, tautest
Opposite
slack, loose
Contrast
Taut sounds exactly like taught, the past of teach: keep the rope taut; the class was taught by a substitute.

Taut has 2 distinct senses.

1. Stretched tight. (натянутый, тугой)

  1. Pull the line taut and tie it off.
  2. The tent ropes were taut in the wind.
  3. Get the wire tauter or the fence will sag.

2. Tense and tightly controlled — of a face, a mood or a story. (напряжённый)

  1. His face was taut with worry.
  2. It's a taut ninety-minute thriller.

tautological#

Pronunciation
/ˌtɔːtəˈlɑːdʒɪkəl/ · taw-tuh-LAH-ji-kuhl
Русский
тавтологический
Type
classifying
Position
both — a tautological definition, The argument is tautological
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-al from tautology

Saying the same thing twice in different words, so that it adds nothing.

  1. 'Free gift' is tautological — a gift is already free.
  2. His definition is tautological: it uses the word it defines.
  3. The whole argument turns out to be tautological.

tawdry#

Pronunciation
/ˈtɔːdri/ · TAW-dree
Русский
безвкусный, кричащий; неприглядный
Type
evaluative
Position
both — mostly before a noun: tawdry souvenirs
Comparison
-er/-est — tawdrier, tawdriest

Tawdry has 2 distinct senses.

1. Cheap and showy. (дешёвый и кричащий)

  1. The casino is all tawdry gold and neon.
  2. The shop sells tawdry souvenirs to tourists.

2. Sordid — morally ugly and a little shabby. (неприглядный, грязный)

  1. The paper printed every tawdry detail.
  2. The story got tawdrier with every new witness.

tawny#

Pronunciation
/ˈtɔːni/ · TAW-nee
Русский
рыжевато-коричневый
Type
color
Position

Comparison

Of a warm brownish-orange color, like a lion's coat.

  1. A tawny lion lay in the shade.
  2. The hills turn tawny by August.
  3. Her hair looked tawnier in the summer light.

tax-exempt#

Pronunciation
/ˌtæksɪɡˈzɛmpt/ · taks-ig-ZEMPT
Русский
освобождённый от налогов
Type
compound; also classifying
Position
both — a tax-exempt charity, The foundation is tax-exempt
Comparison
not gradable
Form
compound — noun + exempt
Contrast
Tax-exempt describes the organization or income that the law excuses from tax; tax-free describes money or goods you pay no tax on: a tax-exempt charity, tax-free shopping.

Not required to pay tax, or not taxed.

  1. The foundation is tax-exempt.
  2. Interest on those bonds is tax-exempt.
  3. Churches hold tax-exempt status.

tax-free#

Pronunciation
/ˌtæksˈfriː/ · taks-FREE
Русский
не облагаемый налогом
Type
compound
Position
both — a tax-free weekend, The withdrawal is tax-free
Comparison
not gradable
Form
compound — noun + -free, meaning without
Contrast
In compounds -free means without, not at no cost: tax-free is без налога, not бесплатный. It is also an adverb: You can take the money out tax-free.

With no tax to pay on it.

  1. The state has a tax-free weekend in August.
  2. Withdrawals after age 59 are tax-free.
  3. They advertise tax-free shopping for tourists.

taxable#

Pronunciation
/ˈtæksəbəl/ · TAK-suh-buhl
Русский
облагаемый налогом
Type
classifying
Position
both — taxable income, The prize is taxable
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-able from tax
Opposite
tax-free, tax-exempt

That tax must be paid on.

  1. Your taxable income is what's left after deductions.
  2. Tips are taxable.
  3. The prize money is fully taxable.

taxing#

Pronunciation
/ˈtæksɪŋ/ · TAK-sing
Русский
утомительный, изматывающий
Type
participial; also evaluative
Position
both — a taxing schedule, The trip was taxing
Comparison
more/most — more taxing, most taxing
Form
-ing from tax, in its older sense of to strain
Contrast
Nothing to do with taxes: a taxing week is a hard, tiring one.

Demanding a lot of effort or energy.

  1. It was a taxing week for everyone.
  2. The hike isn't taxing, just long.
  3. She has a taxing job and two small kids.

taxpaying#

Pronunciation
/ˈtæksˌpeɪɪŋ/ · TAKS-pay-ing
Русский
платящий налоги
Type
participial; also compound
Position
attributive only — before a noun: taxpaying citizens
Comparison
not gradable
Form
compound — noun + -ing participle
Contrast
Also written tax-paying. The person is a taxpayer, one word.

Paying taxes.

  1. He says he speaks for ordinary taxpaying citizens.
  2. Taxpaying businesses want a say in the plan.
  3. They're hard-working, taxpaying people.

teachable#

Pronunciation
/ˈtiːtʃəbəl/ · TEE-chuh-buhl
Русский
обучаемый; поддающийся обучению
Type
quality
Position
both — a teachable player, The kid is teachable
Comparison
more/most — more teachable, most teachable
Form
-able from teach

Teachable has 2 distinct senses.

1. Willing and able to learn. (обучаемый, восприимчивый к учёбе)

  1. He's raw but teachable.
  2. Coaches look for teachable players.

2. Able to be taught — of a skill, or of a moment that teaches something. (которому можно научить)

  1. Empathy is teachable, up to a point.
  2. She turned the argument into a teachable moment.

technical#

Pronunciation
/ˈtɛknɪkəl/ · TEK-nih-kuhl
Русский
технический; узкоспециальный
Type
classifying; also quality
Position
both — a technical problem; the report gets technical
Comparison
more/most — more technical, most technical

Technical has 3 distinct senses.

1. Relating to the machines, methods and skills of a particular field. (технический)

  1. The site is down because of a technical problem.
  2. She has the technical skills for the job.
  3. Technical support finally called me back.

2. Needing special knowledge to follow. (узкоспециальный, сложный для неспециалиста)

  1. The paper gets very technical after page ten.
  2. Can you explain it in less technical language?

3. True only by a strict reading of the rules. (формальный, по формальному признаку)

  1. It was a technical violation, but the referee let it go.
  2. He won the fight by technical knockout.

technological#

Pronunciation
/ˌtɛknəˈlɑːdʒɪkəl/ · tek-nuh-LAH-jih-kuhl
Русский
технологический
Type
classifying
Position
both — mostly before a noun: technological change
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ical from technology

Relating to technology and the changes it brings.

  1. Technological change has hit that industry hard.
  2. The company made a big technological leap in two years.
  3. We teach students to keep up with technological tools.

tectonic#

Pronunciation
/tɛkˈtɑːnɪk/ · tek-TAH-nik
Русский
тектонический; коренной (о переменах)
Type
classifying
Position
both — mostly before a noun: tectonic plates
Comparison
not gradable

Tectonic has 2 distinct senses.

1. Relating to the huge plates that make up the earth's crust. (тектонический)

  1. The earthquake happened where two tectonic plates meet.
  2. Tectonic activity built these mountains.
  3. Geologists study tectonic movement along the fault.

2. So large that it changes everything, like the movement of the earth. (коренной, глубинный)

  1. The election marked a tectonic shift in the country's politics.
  2. Streaming caused a tectonic change in how music is sold.

  3. It's a tedious drive, but the road is easy.

temperamental#

Pronunciation
/ˌtɛmprəˈmɛntəl/ · tem-pruh-MEN-tuhl
Русский
капризный, вспыльчивый; ненадёжный
Type
quality
Position
both — a temperamental chef; the printer is temperamental
Comparison
more/most — more temperamental, most temperamental
Form
-al from temperament
Contrast
Not the Russian 'темпераментный' in the sense of lively or passionate; in English it means moody and hard to deal with.

Temperamental has 2 distinct senses.

1. Changing mood suddenly and often. (капризный, неуравновешенный)

  1. The chef is brilliant but temperamental.
  2. He got more temperamental as the deadline got closer.
  3. She's not temperamental; she just says what she thinks.

2. Working well one day and badly the next. (капризный, ненадёжный (о технике))

  1. The old printer is temperamental in humid weather.
  2. Our car is temperamental on cold mornings.

temperate#

Pronunciation
/ˈtɛmpərət/ · TEM-pur-uht
Русский
умеренный (о климате); сдержанный
Type
classifying; also quality
Position
both — a temperate climate; the winters here are temperate
Comparison
more/most — more temperate, most temperate
Contrast
Don't confuse it with temporary, which is about time, or with temperamental.

Temperate has 2 distinct senses.

1. Never very hot and never very cold. (умеренный (о климате))

  1. Oregon has a temperate climate near the coast.
  2. These plants grow best in temperate regions.
  3. Winters in the valley are more temperate than in the mountains.

2. Calm and controlled in what you say or do. (сдержанный, умеренный)

  1. Her response to the criticism was temperate and careful.
  2. He is temperate in everything, from food to spending.

tempered#

Pronunciation
/ˈtɛmpərd/ · TEM-purd
Русский
закалённый; смягчённый, умеренный
Type
participial
Position
both — tempered glass; the glass is tempered
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ed from temper
Contrast
Most often met inside compounds: even-tempered, bad-tempered, hot-tempered, where it means 'having that kind of temper'.

Tempered has 2 distinct senses.

1. Made harder and stronger by heating and then cooling. (закалённый (о стекле, стали))

  1. The shower door is made of tempered glass.
  2. A tempered steel blade holds its edge.
  3. Car windows are tempered so they break into small pieces.

2. Made less extreme by something else. (смягчённый, умеренный)

  1. He gave a tempered response instead of firing back.
  2. The report shows a tempered optimism about next year.

temporal#

Pronunciation
/ˈtɛmpərəl/ · TEM-pur-uhl
Русский
временной; мирской, светский
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only — the temporal order of events
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
temporal means 'having to do with time'; temporary means 'lasting only a short while'. A temporal arrangement is one in time, not a short-term one.

Temporal has 2 distinct senses.

1. Relating to time or to the order of events in time. (временной, относящийся ко времени)

  1. The novel plays with the temporal order of events.
  2. We looked for a temporal link between the two accidents.
  3. Physicists talk about spatial and temporal dimensions.

2. Belonging to this world rather than to religion. (мирской, светский)

  1. The church lost most of its temporal power.
  2. He drew a line between temporal and spiritual authority.

Able to be defended against criticism or attack.

tenacious#

Pronunciation
/təˈneɪʃəs/ · tuh-NAY-shuhs
Русский
упорный, цепкий
Type
quality
Position
both — a tenacious reporter; she is tenacious
Comparison
more/most — more tenacious, most tenacious

Tenacious has 2 distinct senses.

1. Not giving up, even when something is hard. (упорный, настойчивый)

  1. She's the most tenacious reporter on the staff.
  2. It took a tenacious defense to win that game.
  3. He was tenacious about getting the records released.

2. Holding on tightly and hard to get rid of. (цепкий, стойкий)

  1. The weed has a tenacious root system.
  2. That cough is tenacious — it's been three weeks.

Written or said to push one opinion, not to be fair.

  1. The article is well written but deeply tendentious.

  3. Her second book is even more tendentious than the first.

tender#

Pronunciation
/ˈtɛndər/ · TEN-dur
Русский
нежный; мягкий (о мясе); болезненный
Type
quality
Position
both — a tender look; the meat is tender
Comparison
-er/-est or more/most — tenderer, tenderest; more tender, most tender
Opposite
tough (meat); harsh (manner)
Contrast
tender is also a verb (tender your resignation) and a noun (legal tender). The phrase at a tender age means 'very young'.

Tender has 3 distinct senses.

1. Gentle, kind and loving. (нежный, ласковый)

  1. He gave her a tender look across the table.
  2. There's a tender moment near the end of the film.
  3. She spoke to the child in a tender voice.

2. Soft and easy to cut or chew. (мягкий, нежный (о еде))

  1. Cook the beans until they're tender.
  2. This roast came out more tender than last time.

3. Sore and painful when touched. (болезненный, чувствительный)

  1. My ankle is still tender where I twisted it.
  2. The doctor asked if the area was tender.

tenderized#

Pronunciation
/ˈtɛndəraɪzd/ · TEN-duh-ryzd
Русский
отбитый, размягчённый (о мясе)
Type
participial
Position
both — mostly before a noun: tenderized steak

tense#

Pronunciation

Русский

Contrast

Tense has 3 distinct senses.

1. Nervous and unable to relax. (напряжённый, взвинченный)

  1. She gets tense before every flight.
  2. He looked tense all through dinner.
  3. I was more tense about the interview than about the test.

2. Full of worry, so that nobody feels comfortable. (напряжённый (о ситуации))

  1. It was a tense meeting, and it ran two hours.
  2. The room went tense when he walked in.

3. Stretched tight and stiff. (напряжённый (о мышцах))

  1. My shoulders are tense from driving all day.
  2. Her jaw was tense.

Thick has 4 distinct senses.

  1. She speaks with a thick Boston accent.

заставляющий задуматься
Type
compound
Position
both — a thought-provoking film; the talk was thought-provoking
Comparison
more/most — more thought-provoking, most thought-provoking
Form
compound — noun + -ing participle

Making you think seriously about something.

  1. It's a thought-provoking documentary about food waste.
  2. She asked a thought-provoking question at the end.
  3. The talk was short but thought-provoking.

thoughtful#

Pronunciation
/ˈθɔːtfəl/ · THAWT-fuhl
Русский
внимательный, заботливый; вдумчивый; задумчивый
Type
quality
Position
both — a thoughtful gift; that was thoughtful of you
Comparison
more/most — more thoughtful, most thoughtful
Form
-ful from thought
Opposite
thoughtless

Thoughtful has 3 distinct senses.

1. Kind, and careful about what other people need. (внимательный, заботливый)

  1. That was a thoughtful gift.
  2. It was thoughtful of you to call.
  3. He's the most thoughtful person in the office.

2. Careful and serious in thinking about something. (вдумчивый, обстоятельный)

  1. She gave a thoughtful answer to a hard question.
  2. The report is thoughtful and well argued.

3. Looking as if you are deep in thought. (задумчивый)

  1. He went quiet and thoughtful after the news.
  2. There was a thoughtful look on her face.

thoughtless#

Pronunciation
/ˈθɔːtləs/ · THAWT-luhs
Русский
бездумный, невнимательный к другим
Type
evaluative
Position
both — a thoughtless remark; it was thoughtless of him
Comparison
more/most — more thoughtless, most thoughtless
Form
-less from thought
Opposite
thoughtful

Not thinking about how your words or actions affect other people.

  1. It was a thoughtless remark, but he didn't mean any harm.
  2. Leaving the gate open was thoughtless.
  3. She apologized for being thoughtless about the schedule.

threadbare#

Pronunciation
/ˈθrɛdbɛr/ · THRED-bair
Русский
потёртый, изношенный; избитый
Type
quality
Position
both — a threadbare rug; his coat was threadbare
Comparison
more/most — more threadbare, most threadbare
Form
compound — thread + bare: worn until the threads show

Threadbare has 2 distinct senses.

1. Worn so thin that the cloth is almost see-through. (потёртый, вытертый)

  1. He still wears that threadbare corduroy jacket.
  2. The rug by the door is threadbare.
  3. The hotel towels were threadbare.

2. Used so often that it no longer convinces anyone. (избитый, затасканный)

  1. That excuse is getting threadbare.
  2. He repeated the same threadbare argument.

threatened#

Pronunciation
/ˈθrɛtənd/ · THRET-uhnd
Русский
находящийся под угрозой; чувствующий угрозу
Type
participial
Position
both — a threatened species; he felt threatened
Comparison
more/most — more threatened, most threatened
Takes
by + noun — threatened by the new rules
Contrast
threatened is how the person or animal feels; threatening is what causes the fear.

Threatened has 2 distinct senses.

1. In danger of being harmed or of dying out. (находящийся под угрозой исчезновения)

  1. The turtle is a threatened species in this state.
  2. Small farms are threatened by rising costs.
  3. Coastal towns are the most threatened by the storms.

2. Feeling that someone may hurt you or take what is yours. (чувствующий угрозу)

  1. He gets threatened by anyone younger than he is.
  2. She said she felt threatened during the call.

threatening#

Pronunciation
/ˈθrɛtənɪŋ/ · THRET-uh-ning
Русский
угрожающий
Type
participial
Position
both — a threatening letter; his tone was threatening
Comparison
more/most — more threatening, most threatening
Form
-ing from threaten — what causes the fear
Contrast
Compare threatened: a threatening email frightens people, a threatened worker is the one who is frightened.

Threatening has 2 distinct senses.

1. Suggesting that harm is coming. (угрожающий, грозящий)

  1. She got a threatening letter and called the police.
  2. His tone turned threatening.
  3. The dog took a threatening step toward the fence.

2. Looking as if a storm is about to start. (грозовой, предвещающий непогоду)

  1. The sky looked threatening all afternoon.
  2. Threatening clouds moved in over the lake.

three-dimensional#

Pronunciation
/ˌθriːdɪˈmɛnʃənəl/ · three-dih-MEN-shuh-nuhl
Русский
трёхмерный, объёмный
Type
classifying
Position
both — a three-dimensional model; the image is three-dimensional
Comparison
not gradable
Form
compound — three + dimensional; often shortened to 3-D

Three-dimensional has 2 distinct senses.

1. Having height, width and depth, so it looks solid. (трёхмерный, объёмный)

  1. The printer makes three-dimensional models out of plastic.
  2. A globe is three-dimensional; a map is flat.
  3. They built a three-dimensional map of the cave.

2. Believable and fully developed, of a character in a story. (живой, многогранный (о персонаже))

  1. The villain is the only three-dimensional character in the book.
  2. She writes three-dimensional people, not types.

throbbing#

Pronunciation
/ˈθrɑːbɪŋ/ · THRAH-bing
Русский
пульсирующий, ноющий (о боли)
Type
participial
Position
both — mostly before a noun: a throbbing headache; the pain was dull and throbbing
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ing from throb

Throbbing has 2 distinct senses.

1. Hurting in a steady beat, like a pulse. (пульсирующий, ноющий)

  1. I woke up with a throbbing headache.
  2. There's a throbbing pain in my thumb where I hit it.
  3. The ache was dull and throbbing, not sharp.

2. Beating or sounding with a strong steady rhythm. (пульсирующий, ритмично бьющий)

  1. Throbbing bass came through the wall all night.
  2. We could hear the throbbing engines of the ferry.

throwaway#

Pronunciation

Русский

Type

Position

Comparison

Form

Throwaway has 2 distinct senses.

1. Meant to be thrown out after one use. (одноразовый)

  1. The picnic came with throwaway plates and forks.
  2. We live in a throwaway culture.
  3. Cheap throwaway razors fill the shelf.

2. Said quickly and without much thought, though people may still notice it. (брошенный вскользь, случайный)

  1. It was a throwaway line, but it made the whole room laugh.
  2. His throwaway remark about the budget started a fight.

Ticklish has 2 distinct senses.

tied#

Pronunciation
/taɪd/ · TYD
Русский

participial
Position

Tied has 2 distinct senses.

1. Level with the other side, with neither one ahead. (с равным счётом, вничью)

  1. The score is tied at two.
  2. It was a tied game going into the ninth.
  3. They're tied with Houston for first place.

2. Fastened with a knot. (завязанный, привязанный)

  1. His shoes weren't tied, and he tripped.
  2. The bundle came in tied with string.

tied up#

Pronunciation
/ˌtaɪd ˈʌp/ · tyd UP
Русский
занятый; связанный (о деньгах)
Type
participial
Position
predicative only — I'm tied up until four
Comparison
not gradable
Takes
in / with + noun — tied up in meetings, tied up with a client
Contrast
Very common in American offices as a polite 'busy': He's tied up right now means he can't come to the phone.

Tied up has 2 distinct senses.

1. Too busy with something else to be available. (занятый, не могущий освободиться)

  1. I'm tied up until four — can we talk after that?
  2. She's tied up in meetings all morning.
  3. Sorry, he's tied up with a client right now.

2. Held in something so that it cannot be used or moved. (вложенный, связанный (о средствах))

  1. Most of their money is tied up in the house.
  2. Traffic was tied up for two miles.

tiered#

Pronunciation
/tɪrd/ · TEERD
Русский
многоярусный, многоуровневый
Type
participial
Position
attributive only — a tiered cake
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ed from the noun tier, a level or row
Contrast
tiered rhymes with weird, not with tired — one syllable.

Tiered has 2 distinct senses.

1. Built in levels, one above the other. (многоярусный, ступенчатый)

  1. They ordered a three-layer tiered cake.
  2. The hall has tiered seating, so everyone can see.
  3. We planted the slope in tiered beds.

2. Organized in levels that give different amounts or prices. (многоуровневый (о ценах, системе))

  1. The company moved to tiered pricing.
  2. It's a tiered system: basic, plus and premium.

tight#

Pronunciation
/taɪt/ · TYT
Русский
тесный, тугой; плотный; напряжённый (о графике)
Type
quality
Position
both — tight jeans; the lid is too tight
Comparison
-er/-est — tighter, tightest
Opposite
loose
Contrast
tight is also an adverb after a verb (Hold on tight, Sleep tight), but before a participle use tightly: tightly packed.

Tight has 4 distinct senses.

1. Fitting very closely to the body, often too closely. (тесный, облегающий)

  1. These jeans are too tight.
  2. The shoes felt tighter after the hike.
  3. He wore a tight black T-shirt.

2. Firmly fixed and hard to move or undo. (тугой, туго затянутый)

  1. The lid is on too tight.
  2. Make sure the knot is tight.

3. Leaving very little room, time or money. (плотный, напряжённый; ограниченный)

  1. We're working on a tight deadline.
  2. Money is tight this month.
  3. It's a tight schedule, but it can be done.

4. Very close, with almost nothing between the sides. (напряжённый, с минимальным отрывом)

  1. It was a tight game until the last minute.
  2. The polls show a tight race.

both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
More often a noun (He's a traditionalist); as an adjective it describes people and views that hold to the old ways.

  2. He takes a traditionalist view of the ceremony.
  3. Traditionalist parishes kept the older service.

true-false#

Pronunciation
/ˌtruːˈfɔːls/ · troo-FAWLS
Русский
с ответами «верно/неверно» (о тесте)
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only — before a noun: a true-false question
Comparison
not gradable
Form
compound — true + false

Of a test question that you answer by marking it either true or false.

  1. The quiz is twenty true-false questions.
  2. I'd rather have a true-false test than an essay.
  3. Each true-false item is worth one point.

true-to-life#

Pronunciation
/ˌtruː tə ˈlaɪf/ · troo-tuh-LYF
Русский
правдоподобный, реалистичный
Type
compound; also evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more true-to-life, most true-to-life
Contrast
Hyphenated before a noun (a true-to-life story), usually written open after a verb (the story is true to life).

Showing people and events the way they really are.

  1. It's a true-to-life portrait of a small town.
  2. The dialogue is remarkably true-to-life.
  3. Her novel is more true-to-life than the movie version.

trumped-up#

Pronunciation
/ˌtrʌmptˈʌp/ · truhmpt-UHP
Русский
сфабрикованный, ложный (об обвинениях)
Type
compound; also participial
Position
attributive only — before a noun: trumped-up charges
Comparison
not gradable
Form
compound — from the phrasal verb trump up 'invent'

Invented in order to accuse someone; not real.

  1. He was jailed on trumped-up charges.
  2. The whole case rests on trumped-up evidence.
  3. They fired her for some trumped-up reason about expenses.

truncated#

Pronunciation

Cut short; made shorter by cutting off the end.

  1. We played a truncated version of the game.
  2. The file names come out truncated on that screen.
  3. Rain gave us a truncated season.

trusted#

Pronunciation
/ˈtrʌstɪd/ · TRUHS-tid
Русский
доверенный, надёжный
Type
participial
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
more/most — more trusted, most trusted
Form
-ed from trust
Contrast
Trusted means other people trust you; trusting means you trust other people easily.

That people rely on and believe.

  1. She's his most trusted adviser.
  2. Get your news from a trusted source.
  3. Only a few trusted employees have the key.

trusting#

Pronunciation
/ˈtrʌstɪŋ/ · TRUHS-ting
Русский
доверчивый
Type
participial; also quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more trusting, most trusting
Form
-ing from trust
Contrast
A trusting child believes everyone; a trusted friend is one everyone believes.

Ready to believe that other people are honest and mean no harm.

  1. He's too trusting for his own good.
  2. She gave me a trusting look and handed over the money.
  3. Kids are more trusting than adults.

trustworthy#

Pronunciation
/ˈtrʌstˌwɜːrði/ · TRUHST-wur-thee

Type

Form

Opposite

Honest and reliable, so that people can trust you.

  1. She needed a trustworthy babysitter.
  2. Is that website trustworthy?
  3. He's the most trustworthy person I know.

trusty#

Pronunciation
/ˈtrʌsti/ · TRUHS-tee
Русский
верный, испытанный (о вещи)
Type
quality
Position
attributive only — before a noun: my trusty pocketknife
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-y from trust
Contrast
Half-joking, and used of old reliable objects; a person is trustworthy. Also a noun in prisons (a trusty).

Old and reliable — said of a thing you have depended on for years.

  1. He fixed the fence with his trusty hammer.
  2. I took notes in my trusty little notebook.
  3. She drove out there in the trusty old pickup.

truthful#

Pronunciation
/ˈtruːθfəl/ · TROOTH-fuhl
Русский
правдивый, честный
Type
evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more truthful, most truthful
Takes
about + noun or -ing
Form
-ful from truth
Opposite
untruthful
Contrast
A person is truthful; a statement is true.

Truthful has 2 distinct senses.

1. Telling the truth; not lying. (правдивый, честный)

  1. Just be truthful with me.
  2. She wasn't truthful about where she'd been.

2. Giving a true account of how something really is. (соответствующий действительности)

  1. It's a truthful description of what the job is like.
  2. The movie is more truthful than the book.

trying#

Pronunciation
/ˈtraɪɪŋ/ · TRY-ing
Русский
тяжёлый, трудный, утомительный
Type
participial; also evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more trying, most trying
Form
-ing from try 'test someone's patience'
Contrast
Nothing to do with trying to do something — the adjective means 'hard to put up with'.

Hard to bear; testing your patience.

  1. It's been a trying week.
  2. Those last few months were trying for everyone.
  3. She stayed calm under the most trying conditions.

tsarist#

Pronunciation
/ˈzɑːrɪst/ · ZAHR-ist
Русский
царский
Type
classifying; also origin
Position
attributive only — before a noun: tsarist Russia
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ist from tsar
Contrast
Also spelled czarist, which is the commoner spelling in American books.

Of the emperors who ruled Russia before 1917.

  1. The building dates from tsarist times.
  2. Tsarist Russia had no parliament until 1906.
  3. He collects tsarist coins and medals.

Informal and a little rude; heavy or overweight is the polite word.

Short and fat, in a mild, informal way.

  1. A tubby little dog waddled past.

  3. I felt tubby in that sweater.

Tudor#

Pronunciation
/ˈtuːdər/ · TOO-dur
Русский
тюдоровский
Type
origin; also classifying
Position
attributive only — before a noun: a Tudor house
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Also the family name of the English kings and queens from 1485 to 1603.

Of the Tudor kings and queens of England, or built in the dark-beamed style of that time.

  1. They bought a Tudor house with dark beams across the front.
  2. The class is reading a play from the Tudor period.
  3. Tudor England was smaller than you'd think.

tumultuous#

Pronunciation
/tuːˈmʌltʃuəs/ · too-MUHL-choo-uhs
Русский
бурный, беспокойный; шумный
Type
quality
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
more/most — more tumultuous, most tumultuous

Tumultuous has 2 distinct senses.

1. Full of noise, confusion and sudden change. (бурный, беспокойный)

  1. It was a tumultuous decade in American politics.
  2. Their marriage was short and tumultuous.

2. Very loud and excited — of a crowd or its noise. (бурный (об аплодисментах, встрече))

  1. She got a tumultuous welcome at the airport.
  2. The speech ended to tumultuous applause.

Contrast
Also a noun for a person from Tunisia: she's a Tunisian, the Tunisians.

From Tunisia, or having to do with Tunisia.

  1. We ate at a Tunisian place near the station.

  3. Her husband is Tunisian.

turbulent#

Pronunciation
/ˈtɜːrbjələnt/ · TUR-byuh-luhnt
Русский
бурный, неспокойный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more turbulent, most turbulent

Turbulent has 2 distinct senses.

1. Full of trouble, conflict and sudden change. (бурный, неспокойный)

  1. He grew up during a turbulent period in the country's history.
  2. Their relationship has been turbulent from the start.

2. Of air or water: moving violently and unevenly. (бурный, неспокойный (о воздухе, воде))

  1. The plane hit turbulent air over the mountains.
  2. The river is turbulent for a few days after a storm.

turgid#

Pronunciation
/ˈtɜːrdʒɪd/ · TUR-jid
Русский
напыщенный (о стиле); вздутый, опухший
Type
quality
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
more/most — more turgid, most turgid

Turgid has 2 distinct senses.

1. Of writing or speech: heavy, pompous and hard to get through. (напыщенный, тяжеловесный)

  1. The report is two hundred pages of turgid prose.
  2. His speeches are turgid and far too long.

2. Swollen with liquid. (вздутый, опухший)

  1. The plant cells were turgid with water.
  2. The bite left the skin red and turgid.

From Tuscany in Italy, or done in the style of that region.

twin#

Pronunciation
/twɪn/ · TWIN
Русский
сдвоенный, парный; близнец (о брате, сестре)
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only — before a noun: twin sisters, twin engines
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Much commoner as a noun (she has a twin). Before a noun it is the adjective.

Twin has 2 distinct senses.

1. Born at the same time from the same mother. (-близнец (брат, сестра))

  1. Her twin brothers are in the same class.
  2. The twin girls still dress alike.

2. Being one of two matching things. (сдвоенный, парный)

  1. The room has two twin beds.
  2. The plane has twin engines.
  3. The twin towers of the cathedral are visible from the highway.

Shining with small, quick changes in brightness.

twisted#

Pronunciation
/ˈtwɪstɪd/ · TWIS-tid
Русский
скрученный, погнутый; извращённый
Type
participial
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more twisted, most twisted
Form
-ed from twist

Twisted has 2 distinct senses.

1. Bent or turned out of its proper shape. (скрученный, погнутый)

  1. The frame was twisted in the crash.
  2. A twisted piece of wire held the gate shut.

2. Strange and unpleasant in a cruel way. (извращённый, нездоровый)

  1. That's a twisted thing to say.
  2. He has a twisted sense of humor.
  3. The plot gets more twisted with every episode.

twisty#

Pronunciation
/ˈtwɪsti/ · TWIS-tee
Русский
извилистый; запутанный
Type
shape; also quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est — twistier, twistiest
Form
-y from twist

Twisty has 2 distinct senses.

1. Having many bends. (извилистый)

  1. It's a narrow, twisty road up the mountain.
  2. The trail gets twistier near the top.

2. Of a story: full of unexpected turns. (запутанный, с неожиданными поворотами)

  1. It's a twisty thriller that keeps you guessing.
  2. The plot is twisty but it holds together.

two-dimensional#

Pronunciation
/ˌtuː dɪˈmɛnʃənəl/ · too-di-MEN-shuh-nuhl
Русский
двумерный, плоский; поверхностный (о персонаже)
Type
compound; also classifying
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more two-dimensional, most two-dimensional
Form
compound — two + dimensional

Two-dimensional has 2 distinct senses.

1. Flat — having length and width but no depth. (двумерный, плоский)

  1. A drawing is two-dimensional; a sculpture isn't.
  2. The map gives you a two-dimensional version of a round earth.

2. Of a character in a book or movie: shallow, with no real inner life. (плоский, поверхностный)

  1. The villain is completely two-dimensional.
  2. Even the minor characters are less two-dimensional than in the first book.

Saying one thing to someone's face and another behind their back.

  1. I didn't realize how two-faced he was.
  2. She called her boss a two-faced liar.
  3. That was a two-faced thing to do.

two-sided#

Pronunciation
/ˌtuːˈsaɪdɪd/ · too-SY-did
Русский
двусторонний; имеющий две стороны
Type
compound
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
not gradable
Form
compound — two + side + -ed

Two-sided has 2 distinct senses.

1. Having two sides that are both used or printed on. (двусторонний)

  1. Use the two-sided setting to save paper.
  2. The sign is two-sided, so you can read it from either direction.

2. Having two points of view that both deserve a hearing. (имеющий две стороны (о споре))

  1. Every argument is two-sided.
  2. It's a two-sided story, and we've heard only one half.

Cheating on a wife, husband or partner by secretly seeing someone else.

  1. She threw the two-timing jerk out of the house.
  2. It's a country song about a two-timing boyfriend.
  3. He got a reputation as a two-timing husband.

two-way#

Pronunciation
/ˌtuːˈweɪ/ · too-WAY
Русский
двусторонний; обоюдный
Type
compound; also classifying
Position
attributive only — before a noun: a two-way street
Comparison
not gradable
Form
compound — two + way
Contrast
A two-way street is also an idiom for something both sides have to do: respect is a two-way street.

Two-way has 2 distinct senses.

1. Working or moving in both directions. (двусторонний)

  1. It's a two-way street, so watch for oncoming cars.
  2. The crew talked on two-way radios.

2. Involving two people or sides equally. (обоюдный)

  1. Trust is a two-way deal.
  2. It has to be a two-way conversation, not a lecture.

ultimate#

Pronunciation

Contrast
Also a noun in the ultimate in + noun: the ultimate in comfort.

Ultimate has 2 distinct senses.

1. Final; coming at the end of a process. (окончательный, конечный)

  1. The ultimate decision is hers.
  2. Our ultimate goal is to get everyone housed.

2. The best or greatest of its kind. (высший, идеальный)

  1. It's the ultimate road trip.
  2. That's the ultimate compliment, coming from her.

umpteen#

Pronunciation
/ˌʌmpˈtiːn/ · uhmp-TEEN
Русский
бесчисленный, уйма
Type
quantity
Position
attributive only — before a noun: umpteen times
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Informal, and always a vague 'a lot' — you never say how many, and you cannot count them.

A large but uncounted number of.

  1. I've told you umpteen times to close the door.
  2. There are umpteen ways to do this.
  3. She has umpteen cousins in Ohio.

umpteenth#

Position

Comparison
not gradable
Form

Contrast
Nearly always in for the umpteenth time, said in exasperation.

Coming after a large, uncounted number of others.

  1. For the umpteenth time, no.
  2. This is the umpteenth meeting about the same thing.
  3. He was watching the umpteenth rerun of that show.

un- + American
Contrast
A political insult, not a neutral word: it means 'against American values', not 'not from America'.

Against what the speaker takes to be American values.

  1. He called the protest un-American.
  2. Censorship is un-American, they argued.
  3. Nothing is more un-American than telling people how to pray.

Type
evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Form
un- + beatable

Unbeatable has 2 distinct senses.

1. Impossible to defeat. (непобедимый)

  1. At home, that team is unbeatable.
  2. She had an unbeatable record that season.

2. Better than anything else — of a price, an offer or a spot. (непревзойдённый, вне конкуренции)

  1. They advertise unbeatable prices on tires.
  2. The location is unbeatable.

unbeaten#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈbiːtən/ · uhn-BEE-tuhn
Русский
непобеждённый, не знающий поражений
Type
participial
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Form
un- + beaten
Contrast
Unbeaten is a fact — nobody has beaten them yet; unbeatable is a judgment — nobody can.

Having lost no games or races so far.

  1. They're unbeaten going into the playoffs.
  2. It's the school's first unbeaten season in thirty years.
  3. His record in that weight class is still unbeaten.

unbecoming#

Pronunciation
/ˌʌnbɪˈkʌmɪŋ/ · uhn-bi-KUH-ming
Русский
неподобающий, недостойный
Type
evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more unbecoming, most unbecoming
Takes
in or to + noun
Form
un- + becoming
Contrast
In the military phrase conduct unbecoming an officer it follows the noun and takes no preposition. The older sense 'not flattering' (an unbecoming hat) now sounds dated.

Not suitable for a person in that position; slightly shameful.

  1. That kind of sniping is unbecoming in a senator.
  2. He was fired for conduct unbecoming a teacher.
  3. There was an unbecoming eagerness in the way he agreed.

Takes

Form

Contrast

Without a particular person knowing about it.

  2. The meeting had been moved, unbeknownst to half the staff.
  3. She had been paying the bill for years, unbeknownst to him.

/ˌʌnkəˈnɛktɪd/ · uhn-kuh-NEK-tid
Русский
не связанный, не имеющий отношения
Type
participial
Position
both — mostly after a verb
Comparison
not gradable
Takes
to or with + noun
Form
-ed from connect

Having no link to something else.

  1. The two cases are unconnected.
  2. His firing was unconnected to the audit.
  3. She raised a string of unconnected complaints.

unconscionable#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈkɑːnʃənəbəl/ · uhn-KAHN-shuh-nuh-buhl
Русский
бессовестный, недопустимый
Type
evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more unconscionable, most unconscionable
Form
un- + an old adjective from conscience
Contrast
Formal and strong. In law an unconscionable contract is one so unfair that a court will not enforce it.

So unfair or so wrong that no decent person could accept it.

  1. Charging that much for insulin is unconscionable.
  2. The delay put an unconscionable burden on families.
  3. It would be unconscionable to look the other way.

unconscious#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈkɑːnʃəs/ · uhn-KAHN-shuhs
Русский
без сознания; неосознанный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable — the 'not aware' sense takes more/most
Takes
of + noun, in the 'not aware' sense
Form
un- + conscious
Opposite
conscious
Contrast
For the hidden part of the mind everyday American English says subconscious: a subconscious fear.

Unconscious has 3 distinct senses.

1. Not awake and not able to feel or think, usually after an injury. (без сознания)

  1. The driver was unconscious when the medics arrived.
  2. She hit her head and was unconscious for a minute.

2. Happening without the person knowing it. (неосознанный, бессознательный)

  1. The training is about unconscious bias.
  2. Unconscious habits are the hardest ones to change.

3. Not aware of something. (не сознающий, не замечающий)

  1. He was unconscious of the effect his words had.
  2. They went on talking, unconscious of being overheard.

  3. An unconstitutional statute cannot be enforced.

Uncontrolled means nobody is controlling it; uncontrollable means nobody can.

Impossible to control or stop.

uncooked#

Form
-ed from cook
Opposite

Contrast
For meat and fish Americans usually say raw; uncooked is the normal word for rice, pasta and dough.

Not cooked yet.

  1. Measure the rice uncooked, not after it swells.
  2. Don't eat uncooked cookie dough.
  3. The center of the chicken was still uncooked.

uncool#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈkuːl/ · uhn-KOOL
Русский
немодный; некрасивый (о поступке)
Type
evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more uncool, most uncool
Form
un- + cool
Opposite
cool
Contrast
Informal and mostly spoken. The second sense is about behavior, not fashion.

Uncool has 2 distinct senses.

1. Not fashionable; embarrassing to be seen with. (немодный, несолидный)

  1. Cargo shorts were uncool for about a decade.
  2. He was sure his mom's minivan made him uncool.

2. Not an okay thing to do to someone. (некрасивый, нечестный)

  1. Reading her texts was really uncool.
  2. It's uncool to bail on your friends at the last minute.

uncoordinated#

Pronunciation
/ˌʌnkoʊˈɔːrdɪneɪtɪd/ · uhn-koh-OR-dih-nay-tid
Русский

Position

Comparison

Form

Uncoordinated has 2 distinct senses.

1. Clumsy; bad at controlling your own movements. (неуклюжий, неловкий)

  1. I was the most uncoordinated kid in gym class.
  2. He's tall and a little uncoordinated.

2. Not organized so that the parts work together. (несогласованный, нескоординированный)

  1. The relief effort was slow and uncoordinated.
  2. Uncoordinated agencies did the same work twice.

uncut#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈkʌt/ · uhn-KUHT

Type

Position

Comparison

Form

Uncut has 2 distinct senses.

1. Shown or printed in full, with nothing taken out. (полный, без сокращений)

  1. The DVD has the uncut version of the film.
  2. They aired his speech uncut.

2. Not cut, trimmed or shaped. (необрезанный, неогранённый)

  1. He bought an uncut diamond at an estate sale.
  2. The lawn was uncut for a month.

undecided#

Form

Contrast
In politics the undecided is also a noun: the undecideds broke late.

Undecided has 2 distinct senses.

1. Not having made a choice yet. (не определившийся)

  1. I'm still undecided about the trip.
  2. Ten percent of voters are undecided.
  3. She was undecided whether to major in biology.

2. Not settled or finished. (нерешённый, неопределившийся)

  1. The game was undecided until the last minute.
  2. The question of who pays is still undecided.

under fire#

Pronunciation
/ˌʌndər ˈfaɪər/ · uhn-dur FY-ur
Русский
под огнём критики; под обстрелом
Type
quality — about being attacked or criticized
Position
predicative only — after be, come, fall: the mayor came under fire
Comparison
not gradable
Takes
for + noun or -ing; from + the people attacking
Form
fixed phrase — never before a noun in American English

Under fire has 2 distinct senses.

1. Being strongly criticized. (под огнём критики)

  1. The airline is under fire for canceling flights.
  2. She came under fire from both parties.
  3. He stayed calm while under fire from reporters.

2. Being shot at. (под обстрелом)

  1. The unit was under fire for three hours.
  2. Medics worked under fire to reach the wounded.

A little sick, but not seriously ill.

  1. I'm feeling a little under the weather today.
  2. She was under the weather all last week.
  3. He called in and said he was under the weather.

Unemployed is having no job at all; underemployed is having too little work, or work below your training.

underground#

Pronunciation
/ˈʌndərɡraʊnd/ · UHN-dur-grownd
Русский
подземный; подпольный
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only — before a noun; after a verb it is the adverb, stressed at the end: the pipes run underground
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
The American word for the London Underground is the subway.

Underground has 2 distinct senses.

1. Below the surface of the ground. (подземный)

  1. The building has an underground parking garage.
  2. An underground stream feeds the pond.

2. Secret, or outside the official mainstream. (подпольный, андеграундный)

  1. They ran an underground newspaper for a year.
  2. The band came out of the underground punk scene.

understandable#

Pronunciation
/ˌʌndərˈstændəbəl/ · uhn-dur-STAN-duh-buhl
Русский
понятный; объяснимый
Type

Position

Comparison

Form

Understandable has 2 distinct senses.

1. Natural and easy to sympathize with. (объяснимый, естественный)

  1. Her anger was completely understandable.
  2. It's understandable that he wants more time.
  3. That's an understandable mistake for a first-year teacher.

2. Able to be understood. (понятный, доходчивый)

  1. Write the instructions so they're understandable to a beginner.
  2. The line was bad and he was barely understandable.

uneven#

Pronunciation

Position

Comparison

Form

Opposite

Contrast

Uneven has 3 distinct senses.

1. Not flat or smooth. (неровный, ухабистый)

  1. Watch your step — the sidewalk is uneven.

2. Not the same all the way through; varying in quality. (неравномерный, неровный (по качеству))

  1. The album is uneven — two great songs and a lot of filler.
  2. His play this season has been uneven.

3. Not equal; one-sided. (неравный, неравноценный)

  1. It was an uneven contest from the start.
  2. The workload is uneven across the team.

  1. The children were found unharmed.
  2. He walked away unharmed.
  3. The painting came through the fire unharmed.

unhealthy#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈhɛlθi/ · uhn-HEL-thee
Русский
вредный для здоровья; нездоровый
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
-er/-est or more/most — unhealthier, unhealthiest; also more unhealthy
Form
un- + healthy
Opposite
healthy

Unhealthy has 3 distinct senses.

1. Bad for your health. (вредный для здоровья)

  1. Fried food every day is unhealthy.
  2. The air in the valley is unhealthier in August.
  3. He has a lot of unhealthy habits.

2. Not in good health; looking sick. (нездоровый, болезненный)

  1. The plant looks unhealthy.
  2. She had an unhealthy gray color.

3. Not normal or not good for you — of an interest or a relationship. (нездоровый (об интересе, отношениях))

  1. He has an unhealthy obsession with the case.
  2. It was an unhealthy relationship from the start.

unheard-of#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈhɜːrd ʌv/ · uhn-HURD-uv
Русский
неслыханный, невиданный
Type
compound
Position
both — before a noun (an unheard-of price) or after a verb (It is not unheard-of)
Comparison
not gradable
Form
compound — un- + heard of
Contrast
Keep the hyphen before a noun (an unheard-of delay); after a verb both unheard of and unheard-of are written.

So unusual that it has never happened before.

  1. A rookie winning the title was unheard-of.
  2. They paid an unheard-of price for the house.
  3. Snow in May is not unheard-of here.

Unholy has 2 distinct senses.

1. Shockingly bad or shockingly great. Informal, in fixed phrases. (жуткий, невообразимый)

  1. The kitchen was an unholy mess.
  2. They made an unholy racket until two in the morning.

2. Wicked; not sacred. (нечестивый, богопротивный)

  1. The two parties formed an unholy alliance.
  2. Unholy practices were denounced from the pulpit.

unincorporated#

Unincorporated has 2 distinct senses.

  1. It is an unincorporated nonprofit run by volunteers.
  2. An unincorporated business gives the owner no liability protection.

  3. Half the boxes in the garage are still unlabeled.

unlawful#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈlɔːfəl/ · uhn-LAW-fuhl
Русский
незаконный, противоправный
Type
classifying
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Form
un- + lawful
Opposite
lawful, legal
Contrast
Illegal is the everyday word. Unlawful belongs to legal language and to fixed phrases: unlawful entry, unlawful detention.

Against the law.

  1. The court found the search unlawful.
  2. He was charged with unlawful possession of a firearm.
  3. Firing someone for reporting a safety problem is unlawful.

unleaded#

Pronunciation

Position

Comparison

Form

Contrast
The lead here is the metal, said like led, so the word rhymes with shredded. At the pump Americans also use it as a noun: fill it up with unleaded.

Made without added lead, said of gasoline.

  1. Most cars built after 1975 need unleaded gas.
  2. The station sells three grades of unleaded fuel.
  3. Older engines were never designed to run on unleaded gasoline.

unlisted#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈlɪstɪd/ · uhn-LIS-tid

Position

Comparison

Form

Contrast
An unlisted phone number is one the phone company keeps out of the directory; the British term is ex-directory.

Unlisted has 2 distinct senses.

1. Kept out of a public directory or list. (не внесённый в справочник, незарегистрированный)

  1. Her number is unlisted, so you'll have to email her.
  2. The house is unlisted, which is why it never came up in my search.

2. Visible only to people who have the link, said of a video or a page. (доступный только по ссылке, скрытый из поиска)

  1. I made the video unlisted so only the family can watch it.
  2. Unlisted posts don't show up in search results.

unplayable#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈpleɪəbəl/ · uhn-PLAY-uh-buhl
Русский
непригодный для игры; неиграбельный
Type
evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Form
un- + playable
Opposite
playable

Unplayable has 2 distinct senses.

1. Impossible to play on, said of a field or a golf lie. (непригодный для игры (о поле))

  1. Three days of rain left the field unplayable.
  2. His ball came to rest in a bush, unplayable.

2. Impossible to play, said of a game, a disc or a recording. (неиграбельный; не воспроизводящийся)

  1. The game shipped so buggy that it was unplayable for a month.
  2. The old DVD is scratched and unplayable.

unpleasant#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈplɛzənt/ · uhn-PLEZ-uhnt
Русский
неприятный
Type
evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more unpleasant, most unpleasant
Form
un- + pleasant
Opposite
pleasant

Unpleasant has 2 distinct senses.

1. Not enjoyable; leaving you with a bad feeling. (неприятный, противный)

  1. There was an unpleasant smell coming from the basement.
  2. That flight was the most unpleasant six hours of my life.

2. Rude or unfriendly, said of a person. (неприветливый, грубый)

  1. The clerk was openly unpleasant to everyone in line.
  2. He can be unpleasant when he hasn't slept.

unpolished#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈpɑːlɪʃt/ · uhn-PAH-lisht
Русский
неотшлифованный; сырой, неотработанный
Type
participial
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more unpolished, most unpolished
Form
un- + polished
Opposite
polished

Unpolished has 2 distinct senses.

1. Not made smooth or shiny. (неполированный, неотшлифованный)

  1. Unpolished floors show every scratch.
  2. The stone is unpolished, so it looks dull until you wet it.

2. Rough; not yet worked into finished shape. (сырой, неотработанный)

  1. The draft is unpolished, but the argument is all there.
  2. His delivery is unpolished, and audiences like him for it.
  3. Her early songs were more unpolished and, some say, better.

unpopular#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈpɑːpjələr/ · uhn-PAH-pyuh-lur
Русский
непопулярный
Type
evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more unpopular, most unpopular
Takes
with + person
Form
un- + popular
Opposite
popular

Disliked by most people.

  1. The new tax was unpopular with almost everyone.
  2. She made the unpopular decision to cancel the trip.
  3. The mayor is more unpopular now than when she took office.

unprecedented#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈprɛsɪdɛntɪd/ · uhn-PRES-i-den-tid
Русский
беспрецедентный, небывалый
Type
emphasizing
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
not gradable
Form
un- + precedented, from precedent 'an earlier case'
Contrast
Stress falls on the second syllable and the vowel is short: uhn-PRES-i-den-tid, never un-pre-SEE-.

Never having happened before.

  1. The city saw unprecedented flooding in October.
  2. She won three titles in one year, an unprecedented run.
  3. Demand for the vaccine was unprecedented.

unpredictable#

Opposite
predictable

Impossible to know in advance; changing without warning.

  1. Mountain weather in April is unpredictable.
  2. He is a good player but unpredictable under pressure.
  3. The market has been more unpredictable than usual this year.

principled

Willing to do wrong things to get what you want.

  2. The whole campaign was unprincipled from the first week.
  3. He is the most unprincipled person I have ever worked for.

unprocessed#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈprɑːsɛst/ · uhn-PRAH-sest
Русский
необработанный; нерафинированный
Type
participial
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
not gradable
Form
un- + processed
Opposite
processed

Unprocessed has 2 distinct senses.

1. Not changed from its natural state, said of food. (необработанный, натуральный)

  1. The diet is built around unprocessed foods.
  2. Unprocessed oats take longer to cook than instant ones.

2. Not yet dealt with, said of paperwork or data. (необработанный, нерассмотренный)

  1. Thousands of claims are still unprocessed.
  2. My refund sat unprocessed for six weeks.

Producing little or nothing useful.

unprofessional#

Pronunciation
/ˌʌnprəˈfɛʃənəl/ · uhn-pruh-FESH-uh-nuhl
Русский
непрофессиональный, недопустимый (о поведении)
Type
evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more unprofessional, most unprofessional
Form
un- + professional
Opposite
professional
Contrast
In American English it is almost always about behavior, not skill. Someone who does poor work is incompetent; someone who shouts at a client is unprofessional.

Not meeting the standards of behavior expected at work.

  1. Yelling at a customer is unprofessional.
  2. The email was unprofessional and a little cruel.
  3. Nothing looks more unprofessional than a typo in the subject line.

Not making money.

unprompted#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈprɑːmptɪd/ · uhn-PRAHMP-tid
Русский

Type

Position

Comparison
not gradable
Form
un- + prompted
Contrast
The adverb unpromptedly exists but is rare; Americans use the adjective where Russian would use an adverb: he offered to help, unprompted.

Done without anyone asking or suggesting it.

  1. He offered to pay, completely unprompted.
  2. An unprompted apology means more than one you had to ask for.
  3. She named all fifty states unprompted.

unproven#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈpruːvən/ · uhn-PROO-vuhn
Русский
недоказанный; непроверенный
Type
participial
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
not gradable
Form
un- + proven
Contrast
Unproven and unproved are both correct, but unproven is far more common in American writing.

Unproven has 2 distinct senses.

1. Not shown to be true. (недоказанный)

  1. The claim is unproven and probably false.
  2. Unproven remedies sell very well online.

2. Not yet tested in real conditions. (непроверенный, необкатанный)

  1. The team is starting an unproven quarterback.
  2. They bet the company on an unproven technology.

Form

Happening without anything being done to cause it.

  1. It was an unprovoked attack on a man waiting for a bus.
  2. The report lists six unprovoked shark encounters last year.
  3. Her outburst was sudden and unprovoked.

unpunished#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈpʌnɪʃt/ · uhn-PUN-isht

безнаказанный

Position

Comparison
not gradable
Form

Contrast
It lives in the pattern go unpunished. Before a noun it sounds wrong: say a crime nobody was punished for.

Not punished.

  1. The vandalism went unpunished.
  2. No good deed goes unpunished.
  3. He can't let an insult go unpunished.

unqualified#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈkwɑːlɪfaɪd/ · uhn-KWAH-li-fyd
Русский
неквалифицированный; безоговорочный
Type
participial
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Takes
for + noun; to-infinitive
Form
un- + qualified
Contrast
Two almost opposite feelings. After a verb it means not trained enough: he is unqualified. Before a noun like success or support it means total: an unqualified success.

Unqualified has 2 distinct senses.

1. Not having the training, skills or right to do something. (неквалифицированный, не имеющий права)

  1. He is unqualified for the job and everyone knows it.
  2. An unqualified aide was left in charge of the class.
  3. She is unqualified to give medical advice.

2. Complete, with nothing held back. (безоговорочный, полный)

  1. The concert was an unqualified success.
  2. She gave the plan her unqualified support.

Certain; impossible to doubt.

  1. Her talent is unquestionable.

unquestioning#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈkwɛstʃənɪŋ/ · uhn-KWES-chuh-ning
Русский
беспрекословный, слепой
Type
participial
Position
attributive only — unquestioning loyalty, unquestioning obedience
Comparison
not gradable
Form
un- + questioning
Contrast
Don't mix it up with unquestionable. Unquestioning loyalty is loyalty that never asks why; unquestionable loyalty is loyalty nobody can doubt.

Accepting something completely, without ever asking why.

  1. The group demands unquestioning loyalty.
  2. He gave the coach four years of unquestioning obedience.
  3. Unquestioning trust in a website is how people get scammed.
