Not among the teams or players on the official ranking list.

unreachable#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈriːtʃəbəl/ · uhn-REE-chuh-buhl

Type

Unreachable has 2 distinct senses.

1. Impossible to get to or touch. (недосягаемый, недоступный)

  1. The nest sits on an unreachable ledge.
  2. The top shelf is unreachable without a stool.

2. Impossible to contact. (недоступный, не выходящий на связь)

  1. She's unreachable until Monday morning.
  2. The server was unreachable all afternoon.

Comparison
not gradable
Form

Contrast
Here read is the past participle, said like red: uhn-RED, never uhn-REED. The related unreadable does use the long vowel.

Not read by anyone.

  1. I have four hundred unread emails.
  2. The report sat unread on his desk for a month.
  3. Mark all the messages as unread.

unreadable#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈriːdəbəl/ · uhn-REE-duh-buhl
Русский
нечитаемый; неразборчивый
Type
evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more unreadable, most unreadable
Form
un- + readable
Contrast
Note the vowel change: unread is uhn-RED, but unreadable is uhn-REE-duh-buhl.

Unreadable has 2 distinct senses.

1. Impossible to make out. (неразборчивый, нечитаемый)

  1. His handwriting is unreadable.
  2. The disk is damaged and unreadable.

2. Too dull or too difficult to get through. (нечитабельный, невыносимо скучный)

  1. The manual is technically accurate and completely unreadable.
  2. It is the most unreadable book on the syllabus.

unreal#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈriːəl/ · uhn-REE-uhl
Русский
нереальный; невероятный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Form
un- + real
Opposite
real
Contrast
In casual American speech unreal is praise: that concert was unreal means it was fantastic.

Unreal has 2 distinct senses.

1. Not real, or too strange to feel real. (нереальный, как во сне)

  1. Everything about that week felt unreal.
  2. The colors in the photo look unreal.

2. Amazing — informal. (потрясающий, невероятный)

  1. The view from the summit was unreal.
  2. That catch in the ninth inning was unreal.

Not sensible about what is actually possible.

  1. The deadline is unrealistic.

  3. Their budget was more unrealistic than the schedule.

unrealized#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈriːəlaɪzd/ · uhn-REE-uh-lyzd
Русский
нереализованный; нефиксированный (о прибыли)
Type
participial
Position
attributive only — unrealized potential, unrealized gains
Comparison
not gradable
Form
un- + realized
Contrast
American spelling ends in -ized; British books print unrealised.

Unrealized has 2 distinct senses.

1. Never achieved or developed. (нереализованный, несбывшийся)

  1. The whole article is about unrealized potential.
  2. His plan for the park is one more unrealized dream.

2. Existing on paper but not yet turned into cash, said of gains or losses. (нефиксированный, бумажный (о прибыли, убытках))

  1. You owe no tax on unrealized gains.
  2. The fund is carrying a large unrealized loss.

unreasonable#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈriːzənəbəl/ · uhn-REE-zuh-nuh-buhl
Русский

Type

Position

Comparison

Takes

Form

Opposite

Unreasonable has 2 distinct senses.

1. Not fair or sensible. (неразумный, несправедливый)

  1. Her boss makes unreasonable demands.
  2. It isn't unreasonable to ask for two days' notice.
  3. He gets more unreasonable the later the meeting runs.

2. Far greater than is fair, said of a price or an amount. (чрезмерный, непомерный)

  1. They charge an unreasonable amount for parking.
  2. The rent increase was plainly unreasonable.

So changed that you cannot tell what or who it is.

unrefined#

Pronunciation
/ˌʌnrɪˈfaɪnd/ · uhn-rih-FYND

Unrefined has 2 distinct senses.

1. Not processed; still close to its natural state. (нерафинированный, неочищенный)

  1. The recipe calls for unrefined coconut oil.
  2. Unrefined sugar keeps more of the molasses.

2. Rough; lacking polish or good manners. (грубый, неотёсанный)

  1. His humor is unrefined but never mean.
  2. She thought their taste in furniture was unrefined.
  3. The earlier draft was more unrefined, and funnier for it.

unrelated#

Pronunciation
/ˌʌnrɪˈleɪtɪd/ · uhn-rih-LAY-tid

Unrelated has 2 distinct senses.

1. Not connected with something else. (не связанный, посторонний)

  1. The two cases are unrelated.
  2. She quit for reasons unrelated to the lawsuit.
  3. On an unrelated note, did anyone fix the printer?

2. Not from the same family. (не родственный, не состоящий в родстве)

  1. The two Smiths in my class are unrelated.
  2. They share a last name but are unrelated.

  3. His critics have been unrelenting.

unruly#

Pronunciation

Unruly has 2 distinct senses.

  1. He pushed his unruly hair out of his eyes.
  2. Unruly curls run in the family.

unsatisfied#

Pronunciation
/ˌʌnˈsætɪsfaɪd/ · uhn-SAT-is-fyd
Русский

Comparison

Takes

Form

Opposite

Unsatisfied has 2 distinct senses.

1. Not pleased, because something fell short of what you wanted. (неудовлетворённый, недовольный)

  1. Customers who are unsatisfied can return the item for a refund.
  2. She left the meeting unsatisfied with the answers.
  3. He was unsatisfied even after a second helping.

2. Not met or filled, said of a need, a demand or a condition. (неудовлетворённый, невыполненный)

  1. There is still a large unsatisfied demand for affordable housing.
  2. The loan will not close while two conditions remain unsatisfied.

Unseasoned has 2 distinct senses.

2. Without experience in a job, especially compared with older colleagues. (неопытный, необстрелянный)

  1. The team started an unseasoned rookie at quarterback.

Unsecured has 2 distinct senses.

  1. Never log in to your bank on an unsecured network.
  2. The ladder was left unsecured in the back of the truck.

unsettled#

Pronunciation
/ˌʌnˈsɛtəld/ · uhn-SET-uhld
Русский

Type

Position
both
Comparison

Form

Unsettled has 3 distinct senses.

1. Not decided or finished. (нерешённый, неулаженный)

  1. The question of who pays is still unsettled.
  2. Several claims remain unsettled after two years.
  3. The law in this area is unsettled.

2. Uneasy and worried, unable to relax. (встревоженный, неспокойный)

  1. The news left me unsettled all afternoon.
  2. The baby was unsettled after the long flight.

3. Of weather, likely to change and turn bad. (неустойчивый (о погоде))

  1. The forecast calls for unsettled weather through the weekend.
  2. Conditions stayed unsettled until Thursday.

unsexy#

Pronunciation
/ˌʌnˈsɛksi/ · uhn-SEK-see
Русский

Type

Position
both
Comparison

Form
un- + sexy
Opposite

Unsexy has 2 distinct senses.

1. Dull, not exciting, and so easy to ignore. (скучный, невыигрышный, непривлекательный (о теме, работе))

  1. Maintenance is the unsexy part of the job that keeps everything running.
  2. It is an unsexy topic, but it decides the budget.
  3. Reliable and boring beats exciting and broken, however unsexy that sounds.

2. Not physically attractive. (непривлекательный, несексуальный)

  1. He complained that the uniform was deeply unsexy.
  2. There is nothing unsexy about confidence.

both — mostly after a verb
Comparison
not gradable
Takes
from or by + noun (unshackled from the rules)
Form
un- + shackled

Freed from something that was holding you back.

  1. Unshackled from the old contract, the band recorded whatever it wanted.
  2. Critics warned about an unshackled presidency.
  3. He played like a man unshackled.

unshakable#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈʃeɪkəbəl/ · uhn-SHAY-kuh-buhl
Русский
непоколебимый, твёрдый
Type
quality
Position
both — mostly before a noun: unshakable faith
Comparison
not gradable
Form
un- + shake + -able
Contrast
Also spelled unshakeable; American writing prefers unshakable. It describes beliefs and feelings, not physical objects.

So firm that nothing can change it.

  1. She has an unshakable belief in her students.
  2. His confidence at the plate is unshakable.
  3. The two men formed an unshakable friendship.

unshaken#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈʃeɪkən/ · uhn-SHAY-kuhn
Русский
непоколебленный, твёрдый
Type
participial
Position
predicative only — his faith remained unshaken
Comparison
not gradable
Takes
by + noun (unshaken by the defeat)
Form
un- + shaken
Contrast
Unshaken says that something did not change under pressure; unshakable says that nothing ever could.

Still as firm as before, in spite of what happened.

  1. Her faith in him was unshaken.
  2. The coach seemed unshaken by the loss.
  3. Support for the plan remains unshaken.

unsightly#

Form

Another -ly adjective, never an adverb. It is about how something looks, not about eyesight.

Ugly to look at.

  3. The new fence is frankly unsightly.

unsigned#

Pronunciation
/ˌʌnˈsaɪnd/ · uhn-SYND
Русский

Type
participial
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
not gradable
Form

Opposite

Unsigned has 2 distinct senses.

1. Without a signature, and so not yet valid. (неподписанный)

  1. The bank returned the unsigned check.
  2. An unsigned note was taped to the door.
  3. The contract is still unsigned.

2. Of a musician or athlete, not under contract with a label or team. (не имеющий контракта)

  1. The festival gives a slot to one unsigned band each year.
  2. He was unsigned until the second week of free agency.

Unskilled has 2 distinct senses.

2. Of work, needing no special training. (не требующий квалификации (о работе))

  1. Unskilled labor pays less than it used to.
  2. Most of the summer jobs were unskilled.

unsophisticated#

Pronunciation
/ˌʌnsəˈfɪstɪkeɪtɪd/ · uhn-suh-FIS-tih-kay-tid
Русский
простой, незамысловатый; наивный

quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more unsophisticated, most unsophisticated
Form
un- + sophisticated
Opposite
sophisticated

Unsophisticated has 2 distinct senses.

1. Of equipment or methods, simple and basic. (простой, примитивный)

  1. The attack used unsophisticated tools that anyone can download.
  2. Their testing was unsophisticated but good enough.

2. Of a person, having little experience of art, fashion or city life. (простодушный, неискушённый)

  1. He played an unsophisticated country lawyer.
  2. I felt unsophisticated next to her friends from New York.

unsound#

Pronunciation
/ˌʌnˈsaʊnd/ · uhn-SOWND
Русский
несостоятельный; ненадёжный, аварийный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more unsound, most unsound
Form
un- + sound
Opposite
sound
Contrast
In legal writing of unsound mind means not mentally able to make decisions. Outside that phrase, do not use unsound about people.

Unsound has 2 distinct senses.

1. Of reasoning or a plan, not based on good judgment or good evidence. (несостоятельный, необоснованный)

  1. The argument is unsound because it rests on one bad number.
  2. Economists called the policy financially unsound.

2. Of a building or structure, not safe or solid. (аварийный, ненадёжный (о постройке))

  1. The inspector found the roof structurally unsound.
  2. They tore down the unsound porch.

unsparing#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈspɛrɪŋ/ · uhn-SPAIR-ing
Русский
беспощадный; щедрый
Type
quality
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
more/most — more unsparing, most unsparing
Takes
in + noun or -ing (unsparing in her criticism)
Form
un- + sparing
Contrast
Two nearly opposite meanings, and only the context tells them apart: unsparing criticism is harsh, unsparing generosity is large-hearted.

Unsparing has 2 distinct senses.

1. Holding nothing back in criticism, and so harsh. (беспощадный, не щадящий)

  1. The review was unsparing about the acting.
  2. She is unsparing in her judgment of her own work.

2. Giving freely and without limit. (щедрый, не жалеющий сил)

  1. He was unsparing with his time when a student needed help.
  2. Their unsparing support kept the shelter open.

unspoiled#

Pronunciation

Contrast
American spelling is unspoiled; British books print unspoilt.

Unspoiled has 2 distinct senses.

1. Of a place, still beautiful because nothing has been built on it. (нетронутый, первозданный)

  1. They camped on an unspoiled stretch of coast.

2. Of a person, not made selfish by money or success. (неизбалованный, не испорченный успехом)

  1. Fame has left her completely unspoiled.

Understood by everyone but never stated out loud.

Unsportsmanlike conduct is the name of the penalty in American football and basketball, so this is the form you hear on TV.

Not fair or not respectful toward opponents.

  2. Taunting the goalie is unsportsmanlike.

unstable#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈsteɪbəl/ · uhn-STAY-buhl
Русский
неустойчивый; нестабильный; неуравновешенный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more unstable, most unstable
Form
un- + stable
Opposite
stable

Unstable has 3 distinct senses.

1. Not firm, and likely to fall or move. (неустойчивый, шаткий)

  1. Do not stand on that ladder, it is unstable.
  2. The shelf is unstable without the back panel.

2. Likely to change suddenly for the worse, said of a situation. (нестабильный, неустойчивый (о положении))

  1. The region has been politically unstable for a decade.
  2. Prices are unstable this quarter.

3. Of a person, having sudden changes of mood or mental state. (неуравновешенный, психически неустойчивый)

  1. The note came from someone clearly unstable.
  2. He grew more unstable in the months before the arrest.

Unsteady has 2 distinct senses.

2. Shaking, or not staying at one level. (дрожащий, неровный)

  1. He poured the coffee with an unsteady hand.
  2. Her voice was unsteady when she read the letter.

Unsupported has 2 distinct senses.

  3. The shop is unsurpassed for repairs of this kind.

unused#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈjuːzd/ · uhn-YOOZD
Русский

Position

Comparison

Form

Contrast
Two words share one spelling. Unused 'not used' ends in a z sound, uhn-YOOZD; unused to 'not accustomed to' ends in an s sound, uhn-YOOST, like used to. After unused to comes a noun or -ing, never a bare verb: unused to working nights.

Unused has 2 distinct senses.

  1. She has three weeks of unused vacation.
  2. The back bedroom sits unused most of the year.
  3. Return any unused portion for a refund.

2. unused to: not accustomed to something. (непривычный, не привыкший (к чему-то))

  1. Unused to the cold, he wore two jackets.
  2. She was unused to being interrupted.

unwieldy#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈwiːldi/ · uhn-WEEL-dee
Русский

Type

Position

Comparison

Form

Unwieldy has 2 distinct senses.

1. Big or awkward, and therefore hard to carry, move or handle. (громоздкий, неудобный)

  1. The mattress was unwieldy on the stairs.
  2. He wrestled the unwieldy box into the trunk.

2. Too large or too complicated to work well. (неповоротливый, чересчур сложный)

  1. The committee has grown unwieldy — twenty-two members now.
  2. The tax rules are so unwieldy that nobody reads them.

unwritten#

Unwritten has 2 distinct senses.

unyielding#

Pronunciation
/ʌnˈjiːldɪŋ/ · uhn-YEEL-ding

Form

Unyielding has 2 distinct senses.

1. Refusing to change your mind or give in. (непреклонный, несгибаемый)

  1. The landlord was unyielding about the rent.
  2. Their position on the deadline has been unyielding.

2. Hard and not bending under weight or pressure. (жёсткий, неподатливый)

up#

Pronunciation
/ʌp/ · UHP
Русский
выросший, поднявшийся; вставший; работающий; истёкший
Type
quality
Position
both — mostly after a verb (Sales are up); before a noun only in fixed uses: the up escalator, an up year
Comparison
not gradable
Opposite
down
Contrast
Up is usually an adverb or a preposition (stand up, up the stairs). It counts as an adjective when it follows be, get or seem: The market is up, Are you up? Before a noun it is rare and fixed.

Up has 4 distinct senses.

1. Higher than before in amount, level or price. (выросший, поднявшийся)

  1. Sales are up eight percent this quarter.
  2. Gas prices are up again.
  3. Attendance was up from last year.

2. Awake and out of bed. (вставший, проснувшийся)

  1. I was up at five this morning.
  2. Are the kids up yet?

3. Working; running normally. (работающий, в рабочем состоянии)

  1. The server is back up.
  2. The site should be up again by noon.

4. Finished, of a period of time. (истёкший, закончившийся)

  1. Time's up!
  2. Your thirty days are up on Friday.

up in the air#

Pronunciation
/ˌʌp ɪn ði ˈɛr/ · uhp in thee AIR
Русский
нерешённый, под вопросом
Type
quality
Position
predicative only — after be, remain or leave
Comparison
not gradable
Form
idiom — up + in the air
Contrast
Only the idiom is an adjective; The kite is up in the air is literal. It means 'not decided yet', not 'in doubt about quality'.

Not decided or settled yet.

  1. Our vacation plans are still up in the air.
  2. Whether they'll move is up in the air.
  3. The whole schedule was left up in the air after the storm.

up to#

Pronunciation
/ˌʌp ˈtuː/ · uhp TOO
Русский
на усмотрение (кого-то); замышляющий; способный справиться
Type
quality
Position
predicative only — always after be or feel
Comparison
not gradable
Takes
a noun or pronoun (up to you, up to the job); -ing (up to driving)
Contrast
Up to is also a quantity word meaning 'as many as' (up to fifty people) and part of verbs like keep up to date. As an adjective it follows be: It's up to you, He's up to something, I'm not up to it today.

Up to has 3 distinct senses.

1. up to someone: that person's decision or responsibility. (на усмотрение, на ответственности (кого-то))

  1. It's up to you where we eat.
  2. Whether we go is up to the weather.
  3. It's up to the manager to approve the refund.

2. Doing something secret, usually something you shouldn't. (затевающий, замышляющий что-то)

  1. What are those two up to?
  2. He's up to something — he's been way too quiet.

3. Well enough or good enough for something. (в состоянии, способный справиться)

  1. I don't feel up to going out tonight.
  2. He just isn't up to the job.

Pronunciation
/ˈʌpstɑːrt/ · UHP-start
Русский
новоиспечённый, дерзкий новичок (о фирме, кандидате)
Type
classifying; also evaluative
Position
attributive only — an upstart airline
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Mostly a noun: He's an upstart (выскочка). Before another noun it means 'new and pushing in where nobody expected it', usually with a hint of disapproval.

New and ambitious, and challenging those already established.

  1. An upstart airline is undercutting the majors.
  2. The upstart candidate finished a close second.
  3. Upstart studios are taking market share from the big ones.

upstream#

Pronunciation
/ˌʌpˈstriːm/ · uhp-STREEM
Русский
расположенный выше по течению; начальный (в производственной цепочке)
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only — after a verb it is an adverb: The dam is two miles upstream
Comparison
not gradable
Opposite
downstream

Upstream has 2 distinct senses.

1. Closer to the source of a river. (расположенный выше по течению)

  1. Upstream towns get their water first.
  2. The upstream side of the dam has silted up.

2. At an early stage of a process or a supply chain. (начальный, на раннем этапе цепочки)

  1. The company sold off its upstream oil operations.
  2. An upstream supplier missed a deadline and the whole line stopped.

uptight#

Pronunciation
/ˌʌpˈtaɪt/ · uhp-TYT
Русский
напряжённый, нервный; зажатый
Type
emotion
Position
both — mostly after a verb
Comparison
more/most — more uptight, most uptight
Takes
about + noun or -ing
Contrast
Informal. It mixes anxiety with being too strict or easily shocked, so Don't be so uptight means 'relax, stop making rules'.

Nervous and tense, often about small things, and unable to relax.

  1. He gets uptight about being on time.
  2. Don't be so uptight — it's just a game.
  3. She's the most uptight person in the office.

uptown#

Pronunciation
/ˌʌpˈtaʊn/ · uhp-TOWN
Русский
относящийся к жилой (северной) части города
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only — an uptown apartment
Comparison
not gradable
Opposite
downtown
Contrast
American usage: in New York uptown is the northern part of Manhattan; in many other cities it is the residential part away from the business district. It is also an adverb (We're heading uptown) and a noun.

In or belonging to the uptown part of a city.

  1. They have an uptown apartment near the park.
  2. The uptown train is running late.
  3. It was an uptown crowd in expensive coats.

upturned#

Pronunciation
/ˌʌpˈtɜːrnd/ · uhp-TURND
Русский
вздёрнутый, обращённый вверх; перевёрнутый
Type
participial
Position
attributive only — mostly before a noun
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ed from upturn

Upturned has 2 distinct senses.

1. Turned or pointing upward. (вздёрнутый, обращённый вверх)

  1. She has a small upturned nose.
  2. A row of upturned faces watched the balloon.

2. Turned upside down. (перевёрнутый)

  1. He sat on an upturned bucket.
  2. Upturned boats lined the beach.

upward#

Pronunciation
/ˈʌpwərd/ · UHP-wurd
Русский
восходящий, направленный вверх
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only — an upward trend
Comparison
not gradable
Opposite
downward
Contrast
The adjective is always upward; the adverb can be upward or upwards (prices moved upward, upwards). Never write an upwards trend.

Moving or pointing toward a higher level.

  1. Home prices are on an upward trend.
  2. There's upward pressure on wages.
  3. The chart shows a steady upward climb since March.

upwind#

Pronunciation
/ˌʌpˈwɪnd/ · uhp-WIND
Русский
наветренный, расположенный против ветра
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only — after a verb it is an adverb: Stay upwind
Comparison
not gradable
Opposite
downwind

On the side that the wind is coming from.

  1. Take the upwind side of the fire.
  2. The upwind boats had the advantage.
  3. Smoke never reaches the upwind end of the camp.

urban#

Pronunciation
/ˈɜːrbən/ · UR-buhn
Русский
городской
Type
classifying
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
more/most — more urban, most urban
Opposite
rural
Contrast
Urban means 'of the city'; urbane means 'polished and sophisticated'. Different words, different stress: UR-buhn and ur-BAYN.

Relating to a city or to city life.

  1. Urban schools face different problems than rural ones.
  2. The county gets more urban every year.
  3. She studies urban planning.

urbane#

Pronunciation
/ɜːrˈbeɪn/ · ur-BAYN
Русский
учтивый, изысканный, светский
Type
evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more urbane, most urbane
Contrast
Not a synonym of urban: an urbane host is smooth and well-mannered, not 'from the city'.

Polite, confident and at ease socially, in a way that suggests wide experience.

  1. He was an urbane host who put everyone at ease.
  2. Her writing is witty and urbane.
  3. The ambassador is charming and urbane.

urgent#

Pronunciation
/ˈɜːrdʒənt/ · UR-juhnt
Русский
срочный, неотложный; настойчивый
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more urgent, most urgent
Takes
that-clause with the base verb (It's urgent that she call us)
Contrast
Russian срочный can also mean 'fast': an order that has to be filled quickly is a rush order or express delivery, not an urgent delivery.

Urgent has 2 distinct senses.

1. Needing to be dealt with right away. (срочный, неотложный)

  1. I got an urgent message from the school.
  2. This is more urgent than the budget report.
  3. Nothing on the list is urgent.

2. Showing that you want something done at once. (настойчивый, требовательный)

  1. There was an urgent note in her voice.
  2. He made an urgent appeal for volunteers.

urinary#

Русский

Type
classifying
Position

Relating to urine and the organs that make and carry it.

  1. She's being treated for a urinary tract infection.
  2. The urinary system includes the kidneys and the bladder.
  3. Urinary problems are common after this surgery.

Uruguayan#

Pronunciation
/ˌjʊrəˈɡwaɪən/ · yuur-uh-GWY-uhn
Русский
уругвайский
Type
origin
Position
both
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Always capitalized. The same word is a noun for a person (two Uruguayans); the country is Uruguay.

From Uruguay, or relating to Uruguay.

  1. We went to a Uruguayan steakhouse.
  2. The Uruguayan team won on penalties.
  3. Her grandparents are Uruguayan.

usable#

Pronunciation
/ˈjuːzəbəl/ · YOO-zuh-buhl
Русский
пригодный, годный к использованию; удобный
Type
quality
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more usable, most usable
Form
-able from use
Opposite
unusable
Contrast
The American spelling is usable; useable is a rarer variant. Note the z sound, YOO-zuh-buhl, though the noun use ends in an s sound.

Usable has 2 distinct senses.

1. In good enough condition to be used. (пригодный, годный)

  1. Only half the field is usable after the rain.
  2. Is any of this lumber still usable?

2. Easy to use, of software, tools and designs. (удобный в использовании)

  1. The new site is far more usable on a phone.
  2. They redesigned the app to make it usable for beginners.

used to#

Pronunciation
/ˈjuːst tuː/ · YOOST too
Русский
привыкший (к чему-то)
Type
quality
Position
predicative only — after be, get or become
Comparison
not gradable
Takes
to + noun, pronoun or -ing (used to the noise, used to getting up early)
Contrast
Two different things share this spelling. Be or get used to + noun or -ing means 'be accustomed to' (привык): I'm used to working nights. Used to + a bare verb is a past habit and never has be: I used to work nights (раньше работал). The classic mistake is I'm used to work here — say I'm used to working here or I used to work here. Both are pronounced with an s sound: YOOST.

Familiar with something because you have done it or had it often.

  1. I'm used to the noise now.
  2. It takes about a week to get used to the altitude.
  3. She isn't used to driving in snow.

useful#

Pronunciation
/ˈjuːsfəl/ · YOOS-fuhl
Русский
полезный, нужный
Type
evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more useful, most useful
Takes
for + noun or -ing; to + a person; to-infinitive (useful to know)
Preposition patterns
useful for
Form
-ful from use
Opposite
useless

Helpful for doing or getting something.

  1. That's a useful tip, thanks.
  2. A second key would be useful for the neighbors.
  3. It's useful to know a little Spanish around here.

useless#

Pronunciation
/ˈjuːsləs/ · YOOS-luhs
Русский
бесполезный, никчёмный
Type
evaluative
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more useless, most useless
Takes
at + noun or -ing in the informal 'bad at' sense (useless at math)
Form
-less from use
Opposite
useful

Useless has 2 distinct senses.

1. Not useful; of no help at all. (бесполезный, ненужный)

  1. A phone with no signal is useless.
  2. It's useless to argue with him.
  3. These old batteries are useless.

2. Very bad at doing something — informal. (никудышный, неспособный (в чём-то))

  1. I'm useless at remembering names.
  2. He's useless in the kitchen.

user-friendly#

Easy to use or understand, especially for people who are not experts.

  1. The new dashboard is much more user-friendly.
  2. We need a user-friendly version of the manual.
  3. Their checkout process isn't very user-friendly.

usual#

Pronunciation
/ˈjuːʒuəl/ · YOO-zhoo-uhl
Русский
обычный, привычный
Type
classifying; also quality
Position
both — mostly before a noun; after a verb usually with it: It's usual to tip
Comparison
more/most — more usual, most usual
Takes
for + noun with a to-infinitive (It's usual for guests to bring something)
Opposite
unusual
Contrast
As usual is fixed (He was late, as usual), and the usual is a noun (I'll have the usual). Don't mix the adjective usual with the adverb usually: my usual seat / I usually sit here.

Happening or done most of the time; normal for this person or situation.

  1. We met at our usual table.
  2. It's usual to tip about twenty percent.
  3. She took the usual route home.

usurious#

Pronunciation
/juːˈʒʊriəs/ · yoo-ZHUUR-ee-uhs
Русский
ростовщический, грабительский (о процентах)
Type
evaluative; also classifying
Position
both — mostly before a noun
Comparison
not gradable
Form
-ous from usury
Contrast
Formal, mostly legal and financial. The noun is usury, YOO-zhuh-ree: lending money at illegally high interest.

Charging interest at an unfairly or illegally high rate.

  1. The lawsuit calls the fees usurious.
  2. Payday lenders charge usurious rates.
  3. The contract was thrown out as usurious.

utilitarian#

Pronunciation
/ˌjuːtɪlɪˈtɛriən/ · yoo-til-i-TAIR-ee-uhn
Русский
утилитарный, практичный; утилитаристский
Type
evaluative; also classifying
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more utilitarian, most utilitarian

Utilitarian has 2 distinct senses.

1. Made to be useful rather than attractive. (утилитарный, чисто практичный)

  1. The kitchen is plain and utilitarian.
  2. They chose utilitarian furniture that could take a beating.

2. Relating to utilitarianism, the idea that the right action brings the most good to the most people. (утилитаристский)

  1. He made a utilitarian argument for the policy.
  2. The debate turned into a cold utilitarian calculation.

utility#

Pronunciation
/juːˈtɪləti/ · yoo-TIL-uh-tee
Русский
универсальный, запасной (об игроке); хозяйственный, рабочий
Type
classifying
Position
attributive only
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
In utility bill, utility company and utility room this is the noun utility used as a modifier. The adjective proper means 'able to fill several roles' (a utility infielder) or 'plain and practical' (utility shelving).

Utility has 2 distinct senses.

1. Able to play several positions or do several different jobs. (универсальный, играющий на разных позициях)

  1. He's a utility infielder who can catch, too.
  2. Every crew needs one utility player who can cover anything.

2. Made for plain practical use rather than for looks. (хозяйственный, рабочий)

  1. We put utility shelving in the garage.
  2. It's a utility knife, not a fancy one.

utmost#

Pronunciation
/ˈʌtmoʊst/ · UHT-mohst
Русский
величайший, крайний, предельный
Type
emphasizing
Position
attributive only — of the utmost importance
Comparison
not gradable
Contrast
Also a noun: She did her utmost. Don't confuse it with uppermost, which is about position. The variant uttermost is literary.

The greatest possible — used to stress how much.

  1. This is a matter of the utmost importance.
  2. Handle the samples with the utmost care.
  3. He has the utmost respect for her.

utopian#

Pronunciation
/juːˈtoʊpiən/ · yoo-TOH-pee-uhn
Русский
утопический
Type
evaluative; also classifying
Position
both
Comparison
more/most — more utopian, most utopian
Opposite
dystopian
Contrast
Capitalized when it points to Thomas More's Utopia, lowercase in ordinary use. It usually hints that the plan is too perfect to work.

Imagining or aiming at a perfect society, often unrealistically.

  1. It's a utopian vision with no plan to pay for it.
  2. The commune started out with utopian ideals.
  3. Critics called the proposal utopian.
